- **The local web view now shows the same agent-rules staleness warning the command line shows.** The web view assembles its own report by calling the same engine the command line calls, but it was not handing that engine the committed rules files — and the engine skips the staleness check entirely when they are not supplied. A project with a drifted, hand-edited, or missing agent-rules digest therefore warned on the command line and showed nothing at all in the browser: the surface a person is most likely to trust read *cleaner* than the truth. Both surfaces now read those files through one shared reader, so neither can report a state the other does not, and the parity test that guards this compares the web view against the command line's full input set rather than a partial one — the same class of omission cannot pass unnoticed again.
- **The two showcase example projects no longer describe their own configuration backwards.** Both opened with a note claiming the project "has no coverage block" — sitting directly above the coverage block it said did not exist. What they actually declare is no required roots, which is what makes the whole tree required; the note now says that.
- **(Repo-internal.)** The repository gate's agent-rules freshness step could not see the one state it most needs to catch. It walked the digest files that already exist, so an example project with no agent-rules file at all was never visited and the step passed — exactly the state a newly added example lands in when the install was never run there. It now walks the example project ROOTS and fails on a missing file, and it asserts up front that the canonical hash it compares against really is a hash: if that hash ever came back empty, a file carrying no anchor would have compared empty-to-empty and passed, and the step would have reported green while checking nothing.
- **The universal installer's empty-directory cleanup no longer refuses to prune a directory whose own name starts with two dots.** The containment guard added with that cleanup rejected any path whose relative form began with `..` — which is the correct test for an escape attempt, but also matched a perfectly legitimate directory literally named `..cache` or `..v2`, leaving it behind instead of removing it. The guard now tests the first path segment for exactly `..`, so only genuine escapes are refused.

## [5.5.1] - 2026-07-06

### Fixed

- **The deterministic worker-thread pool (5.5.0) no longer spawns threads for tiny fill sets.** Each worker pays a one-time parser warmup, so parallelizing a handful of deterministic checks is strictly slower than one warmed in-process parser — and on a constrained or contended machine (e.g. CI running two jobs at once) the repeated warmup could push a small `yg check --approve` past a tight time budget. The pool now engages only when there is enough work to give every worker a meaningful share (at least 8 pairs each); below that it runs in-process, exactly as before the pool existed. Verdicts are unaffected — this only changes when the pool turns on.

## [5.5.0] - 2026-07-06

### Added

- **`yg init` can now bootstrap a fresh graph with no reviewer configured at all — keyless from the first command.** Naming only `--platform` (with no `--provider`) now scaffolds the graph and installs the platform's rules file, writing no `reviewer:` section — legal now that the missing-reviewer check is conditional on an effective judgment rule actually existing. Script rules, dependency checks, and the deterministic CI gate all work immediately with zero keys and zero cost; a judge can be added later with `yg init --provider <name> [--model <m>]`, which now works against a keyless graph exactly like a fresh one. The fresh-repo dispatch was rewritten so flags are authoritative: `--provider` drives the existing judge-configuring bootstrap, `--platform` alone drives the new keyless bootstrap, `--model`/`--endpoint` given without `--provider` is now a clear error (they configure a judge that isn't there), a fully-specified command never opens the interactive wizard even from a terminal, and a non-interactive run naming no platform now fails with guidance instead of silently guessing which agent platform to install (previously it would launch the interactive wizard's prompts against a non-interactive, promptless stream). The two "unknown platform" / "unknown provider" flag-validation checks were consolidated into shared helpers so every dispatch branch validates identically.
- **An already-initialized project can now add or change its reviewer, or switch its platform rules file, entirely from flags — no interactive menu required.** `yg init --provider <name> [--model <m>] [--endpoint <url>]` against an existing `.yggdrasil/` (re)configures the judge; `yg init --platform <name>` switches the installed platform rules file; either flag works alone or combined in one command, and both reuse the same flag+environment resolver and write path as the fresh-bootstrap forms above. The interactive reconfiguration menu (upgrade / configure reviewer / change platform) still opens when neither flag is given in a terminal; with neither flag and no TTY, the command now reports the available flag forms instead of doing nothing silently.
- **Every `yg check --approve` fill now records a local, append-only event line for each verdict or failed attempt.** Until now, a refusal or an infrastructure failure (a reviewer that could not be reached, a check that crashed) left no trace once the run ended — the terminal output was the only record, and a later successful run silently overwrote it. Each completed pair — approved, refused, or one of the specific no-write outcomes (an unreachable reviewer, a crashed check, a malformed suppress marker) — now appends one JSON line to `.yg-events.jsonl` under `.yggdrasil/`, timestamped from the same clock the run already uses. This file is local-only telemetry: gitignored, never committed, and never read back by any check/verify/render path — it exists purely to make fill outcomes observable across runs, motivating future rule-health reporting (which rules refuse often, which infrastructure paths fail repeatedly). A failed append is swallowed and can never affect a fill's outcome.
- **Agents can now onboard a human on demand.** A new knowledge topic — `yg knowledge read onboarding` — is a full tutor playbook: the agent calibrates once, detects the context (a repo the team already governs, a repo without a graph, or no repo at all), teaches through live, zero-trace demonstrations on the user's own code (`yg aspect-test` never writes the lock; a reverted scratch edit leaves the repo byte-identical), and closes with the user's own words, not applause. The agent rules advertise it, so "onboard me into Yggdrasil" works in any adopted repo, in any language.
- **The `yg check` header and the portal's counts panel now split the verified-pair count into deterministic vs LLM-reviewed.** Both previously reported one combined "verified" number, so a fully green build read the same whether every pair was checked for free by a local rule or actually reviewed by an LLM. The PASS/FAIL header now appends `N verified (D deterministic, L LLM)` whenever at least one pair is verified, and the portal's coverage panel shows the identical split next to its existing expected-pair breakdown — both read off the same per-pair tally `yg check` already computes, so the two can never disagree. On a fresh checkout with no local deterministic cache yet, the deterministic count honestly reads 0 until `yg check --approve --only-deterministic` rebuilds it — this is not a bug, those pairs are genuinely unverified.
- **`yg aspect-test --repeat N` measures how consistently an LLM reviewer judges the same rule.** While authoring or sharpening an LLM rule it is easy to ship prompt text so ambiguous the reviewer flips its own verdict from one run to the next — invisible until it causes a flaky build later. Passing `--repeat N` (N of at least 2) re-runs each unit N times against the identical prompt and prints a per-unit `stability: k/N satisfied` line: how often the reviewer returned the same answer. Each run is deliberately reduced to a single vote so the figure reflects the judge's raw self-consistency, and the text is careful never to imply consistency means correctness — a rule can be consistently wrong. The total number of reviewer calls (`repeat N × units`) prints up front so the cost is visible before the first call; runs where the reviewer could not be reached are excluded from the ratio and reported separately; any single run that refuses marks the unit refused, and a unit whose every run failed to reach the reviewer is reported as incomplete rather than passed. The flag is for LLM rules only — it is rejected with the prompt-preview mode, with ad-hoc file lists, and for deterministic rules (which are already exactly reproducible; use `--check-determinism` there).
- **A consensus review now shows its individual vote split, not just the majority verdict.** When an LLM rule runs on a tier configured for consensus greater than one, Yggdrasil takes several independent review passes and keeps the majority. Until now only that final verdict was visible, so a rule that barely won a 2-of-3 majority looked identical to one every pass agreed on. `yg aspect-test` now appends the tally — `[votes 2/3]` — to each unit's line whenever more than one pass was cast, so a borderline rule that only just carried its majority is no longer indistinguishable from a unanimous one; and each recorded fill event now carries the same satisfied-of-total count, so later rule-health analysis can see how close each majority was. The majority verdict itself is unchanged — this only surfaces the split that was already being computed.
- **A `drills/` directory inside an aspect folder is now a reserved place for hand-authored check fixtures and never registers as an aspect.** Aspect discovery now hard-skips any directory named `drills` beneath an aspect, so small synthetic sources kept there to exercise a deterministic check cannot be mistaken for nested aspect definitions. This repository seeds such a dev-set: for three of its deterministic rules, a handful of tiny `.ts` cases whose folder name states the expected verdict (`violates-*` must refuse, `satisfies-*` must pass), run by hand via `yg aspect-test`, so a maintainer sharpening a check can confirm in seconds it still catches what it should. Repository-internal regression fixtures with no effect on the published package; the reserved-directory behavior itself ships to adopters. See `.yggdrasil/aspects/README.md` for the convention and run book.

### Changed

- **Deterministic checks now run across CPU cores during `yg check --approve`, instead of one after another.** The free, local `check.mjs` rules are dominated by source parsing (a CPU cost), so on a repo with many deterministic-checked nodes the fill phase was bottlenecked on a single core. Deterministic pairs now execute across a pool of worker threads sized automatically from the machine's available cores — the free, keyless `yg check --approve --only-deterministic` gate (the one CI and pre-commit run) is where this pays off most. This is a speed change only: verdicts, their content hashes, the deterministic-first ordering, the enforced-refusal-skips-LLM gate, grouped diagnostics, and fail-closed behavior on a crashed check are all byte-identical to the sequential path (a parity test pins this). Nothing to configure. Clarified alongside this: the `parallel` config setting governs ONLY the LLM (reviewer) fill phase — where the cost is network latency — and never applied to deterministic checks; the docs and config schema previously described it as covering "pair verifications" generally, which was misleading.

- **The `init` command's setup logic was split out of its handler into two focused support modules.** The interactive reviewer-provider wizard (provider selection, model fetch, connection test, and persisting the chosen tier plus API key) and the filesystem scaffolding (creating the graph directory structure, writing the default config/architecture templates, installing the platform rules file, and maintaining the git-ignore/attributes) each moved into their own command-layer support module, leaving the command file with only orchestration. It is a pure move — no prompt, error text, or write path changed. To make it possible, the shared command-support type in this repo's own architecture graph was widened so a support helper may call the LLM-shared and template layers that a command's own setup already uses (the type stays deny-by-default; only those two dependency directions were opened). Repository-internal refactor with no effect on the published package.
- **The repository lint gate now covers the test tree, not just `src/`.** `npm run lint` (and the `repo-check.sh` quality gate that calls it) previously linted only `src/`, so lint debt could accumulate in `tests/` unchecked; the gate now runs `eslint src/ tests/`. Two rules are intentionally relaxed for tests only, via a `tests/**` override in the ESLint config: `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` (tests use `any` freely for mock shapes and error-object casts — precise types there are high-churn, low-value) and `no-empty-pattern` (Playwright/vitest worker fixtures require an empty object-destructure first parameter, `async ({}, use) => …`). Every other rule stays fully enforced across the test tree, and the pre-existing debt it surfaced — unused imports and variables, redundant assignments, and hard-to-count literal spaces in regexes — was fixed rather than silenced. This is a repository-internal developer-tooling change with no effect on the published package.

### Fixed

- **Non-interactive `yg init` no longer rejects a missing `--model` for the installed-agent (`claude-code`) provider.** The non-interactive bootstrap path required `--model` for every provider, including `claude-code`, even though the interactive wizard already offers a sensible built-in alias (`sonnet`) for that provider. The flag+environment resolution that turns `--provider`/`--model`/`--endpoint` plus the provider's API-key environment variable into a reviewer configuration is now a single shared function, used by the non-interactive init path: `claude-code` now defaults its model to `sonnet` when `--model` is omitted, while every other provider still requires the model to be named explicitly. The API key is unaffected — it is still read only from the environment, never a flag.
- **Repository-internal refactor — the flag-driven reviewer resolve/persist path is now shared across the fresh and existing-repo non-interactive init flows.** The resolve-and-validate step and the write step (config tier + secret overlay, or the key-missing warning) were extracted into two small helpers reused by both non-interactive `yg init` paths so the two cannot drift; validation deliberately runs before any scaffolding, so a bad flag combination fails without leaving a partial graph directory behind. The `--upgrade` path's missing-graph guard was likewise moved out of the command handler into a shared preamble helper, so the handler no longer inlines the check or its message. No effect on the published package or on any user-visible behavior.
- **A `yg-suppress` marker shown inside a fenced code block in a Markdown file is no longer honored as a live waiver.** A Markdown file has no code grammar, so its markers were found by a raw line scan that could not tell a documented example from a real comment — a page demonstrating the suppress syntax inside a fenced code block (backtick or tilde fences) silently waived that aspect on the line below it. Fenced lines are now treated as documentation and skipped in both the reviewer-honoring path and the `yg suppressions` audit, which share one fence detector so they can never disagree; an unclosed fence is treated as running to the end of the file, and every fence ambiguity resolves toward enforcement — a fenced example is only ever skipped, never honored. This is a behavior change in the stricter direction. To place a genuine, honored waiver in a Markdown file, use an HTML comment outside any fence: `<!-- yg-suppress(<aspect-path>) <reason> -->`. If a Markdown file mapped as reviewed source previously relied on a fenced marker, its local deterministic cache may still hold the old verdict — delete `.yggdrasil/.yg-lock.deterministic.json` and re-run `yg check --approve --only-deterministic` to realign it (free, keyless).
- **The aspect schema reference no longer advertises retired per-tier reference size caps.** `yg schemas read aspect` documented a `references:` byte limit (64 KiB per file, 256 KiB total) configured via `reviewer.tiers.<tier>.references.*` in `yg-config.yaml` — a knob the config parser rejects outright. There is no per-tier reference byte cap; reference bytes simply count toward the assembled reviewer prompt, bounded by the tier's `max_prompt_chars` (default 50000), with an over-limit pair reported as a blocking `prompt-too-large` error. The schema text now describes that real gate instead of the retired one.
- **A missing or malformed business-process flow definition no longer tells you the project isn't set up.** When a flow definition file was absent, was a folder instead of a file, was unreadable, or had a syntax error, `yg` reported "No .yggdrasil/ directory found — run yg init" as if the whole project were uninitialized, or aborted with an internal "please file an issue" error. On an already-initialized project the real fault is one specific flow definition, so the message now names that file, says it is missing or malformed, and tells you to fix or remove it. The genuine not-initialized case (no graph directory at all) keeps its own separate "run yg init" guidance, so neither message masks the other.
- **A rule that only inspects a related component's file names no longer re-checks when those files' contents change.** A deterministic rule that reaches a related component and reads only its file paths — for example, to confirm a matching test file exists — previously tied its recorded result to every one of that component's file contents, so editing any of them forced the rule to re-run even though its outcome could not have changed. Such a rule now records only what it actually reads: inspecting a file's path no longer binds the result to that file's bytes, while a rule that reads a file's contents still re-checks when those contents change. The verdict is unchanged; only the set of edits that trigger a re-check is narrower (and these checks make no API calls, so a re-check is always free).
- **Bare `yg check --top` now renders the single suggested-next group instead of empty section headers.** The flag without a value previously printed `Errors (N):` / `Warnings (N):` headers with no content beneath — counts with nothing to act on. It now behaves as `--top 1` (exactly the group the `Next:` pointer draws from), and any `--top` section whose slice selected no groups gets an explicit note (`(no warning groups within --top 1 — run yg check for the full list)`) instead of a dangling empty header. The true aggregate counts and the real exit code are unchanged, and the contradictory "renders zero groups" wording in the reference docs is gone — every description now states the contract the CLI actually ships.
- **`yg aspect-test` now opens with a verdict stamp, and a refused LLM diagnostic exits 1.** A refusal previously began with a bare file path (the verdict had to be inferred from the list's existence), and the LLM path exited 0 even when the reviewer refused — against the documented exit contract. Every run now leads with `yg aspect-test: refused — N violation(s)` / `yg aspect-test: satisfied — No violations.` (verdict first — also the friendliest shape for screen readers and for agents quoting the result), LLM runs close with an overall stamp, a refused LLM run exits 1, and a run with infra-skipped pairs is stamped `incomplete` with a nonzero exit instead of silently passing.
- **A live `yg aspect-test` no longer reports an unreachable reviewer as a code refusal.** When the reviewer endpoint returned an error or an unreadable response mid-run, the diagnostic stamped the unit `refused` — code-violation language for an infrastructure failure the reviewer never actually judged — which sends an agent editing code the reviewer never saw. Such a unit is now treated as not-yet-verified: the run stamps `incomplete`, prints the infrastructure cause, exits nonzero, and records nothing — matching how `yg check --approve` already classifies the identical failure.
- **`yg aspect-test` error messages now carry the standard `Error:` prefix.** Every one of its bespoke diagnostics — unknown aspect or node, wrong mode, `--dry-run` on a deterministic aspect, reviewer/tier/reference/companion failures, non-determinism — now opens with `Error:`, matching the error-output convention every other `yg` command follows, so a failed diagnostic is unmistakable in logs and to agents keying on the prefix.
- **The aspect-test footer no longer claims something the lock may not show.** The old trailer said "yg check still reports the stored verdict" — false at the exact moment the tool is most used, when a scratch edit is still in the tree and the pair shows as unverified. The footer now reads `diagnostic only — lock unchanged; yg check judges the lock against your files, not this run`, which is true in every state.
- **When writing a deterministic check that walks the AST, a visitor must `return` (not `return false`) on a node it does not match — otherwise the walk stops there and inspects nothing beneath it.** In the `walk(node, visitor)` helper a visitor's `return false` means "skip this subtree." Returning `false` for every node that is not the type you are hunting — including the file's root node, which is never your target — aborts the traversal at the very first node, so the check reaches none of the file's contents and reports every subject clean no matter what it holds. The correct shape is to `return` (or return nothing) on a non-matching node so descent continues, and to `return false` only after handling a matched node whose subtree you genuinely want to skip. A shipped deterministic check hit exactly this trap — it walked for import statements but skipped every non-import node, so it never inspected a single import and silently passed every file; with the corrected traversal it now genuinely catches the imports it was written to guard.
- **`yg`'s own commit-provenance reader now recognizes a linked development worktree.** The reader that stamps a verification run with the current git commit assumed `.git` is always a plain directory; a linked worktree checks out the repository with `.git` as a one-line pointer file to a private per-worktree git directory (which in turn points back to the main checkout for shared branch refs), so the reader silently found nothing and reported no commit ref at all whenever it ran from such a worktree. It now follows the pointer file to the worktree's own git directory for `HEAD`, then follows that directory's own pointer back to the shared location for branch refs, so the reported commit is correct regardless of which checkout the run happens in.
- **Draft aspects can now be exercised with `yg aspect-test` regardless of reviewer kind.** A `status: draft` LLM aspect previously answered "No pairs …" (while a draft deterministic aspect ran fine), silently breaking the documented authoring ladder — author as draft, iterate with aspect-test. Draft keeps its meaning everywhere it matters (no expected pairs, no verdicts, no cost in `yg check` / `--approve`); it just no longer hides the aspect from the explicitly-invoked diagnostic. Note: a live (non-`--dry-run`) aspect-test on a draft LLM aspect now makes a real reviewer call — a single, user-invoked diagnostic, documented as such.
- **Violations without a line number render cleanly.** A deterministic check returning `{ file, message }` (`line` is documented optional) used to render as `src/file.ts:?:` in `yg check` and `L?:` in `yg aspect-test`. The placeholder segment is now simply omitted: `src/file.ts: message`, and in aspect-test the bare message under its file header.
- **`yg check` no longer flags `.git` as an unmapped file in git worktrees.** In a worktree (and in submodule checkouts) `.git` is a small pointer file rather than a directory; the repository scan skipped only the directory form, so every run in a fresh worktree reported one false blocking coverage error — `unmapped: .git` — on an otherwise green repository. The scan now skips `.git` in both forms, at any depth, so a green build stays green in a worktree.
- **A directory-form mapping entry is now classified by the files it owns, like a glob, so a directory mapping under an extension-glob type no longer mis-reports `type-when-mismatch`.** A node whose type's `when` is an extension glob (e.g. `src/**/*.ts`) and whose mapping listed a directory (e.g. `src/emails/`) was matched against the literal directory string — which never matches an extension glob — so `yg check` wrongly refused it, even though `src/emails/**/*.ts` with identical ownership passed. Both forms are documented-valid; every mapping entry is now expanded to the concrete files it owns before the type-`when` check, so classification matches ownership. A file inside the directory that genuinely violates the type's `when` is still flagged by name, and a mapping to a missing file still reports it as unreadable.
- **`yg context --file` on an always-excluded path now says so instead of suggesting a dead mapping.** For a path the coverage scan unconditionally skips — `.git` (directory or worktree/submodule pointer file, at any depth) and the graph's own `.yggdrasil/` internal tree — the command used to answer "has no graph coverage … add it to a node mapping", advice that would map a file `yg check` never enumerates. It now reports the path is excluded from coverage by design with no action needed, and exits 0 (an excluded path is not a coverage gap). An ordinary uncovered source file still gets the mapping suggestion.
- **`yg aspect-test --node` now notes when a deterministic aspect is not attached to the node.** Running a deterministic check against a node the aspect does not apply to is a useful test-before-attach, but it silently printed a verdict `yg check` will never produce (the reviewer path already says "No pairs" in the same case). The deterministic run still happens, but a one-line note now precedes it when the aspect is not effective on the node through any channel, so the printed verdict is not mistaken for a tracked pair. Exit code and verdict output are unchanged.
- **Concurrent writers to the same cache or lock file no longer collide on a shared temporary name.** Two CLI runs, or parallel workers inside one, writing to the same shared cache or lock file previously raced through an identical temporary file name, so one writer's cleanup could pull the temporary file out from under another still writing it, surfacing as a spurious "file not found" error on an otherwise correct file. Each writer now generates its own unique temporary name before the final atomic rename onto the shared file, so only that last swap is ever contended, and a reader always sees the complete old content or the complete new content — never a partial file.
- **A project with no judgment (LLM) rules no longer needs a `reviewer:` section just to pass.** `yg check` used to demand `yg-config.yaml` declare a reviewer tier unconditionally, even when every rule in the graph was a free local check — a script-only project with zero LLM rules was blocked from ever going green without configuring a model that would never be called. The missing-reviewer error now fires only once a judgment rule is actually effective on some component (computed the same way `yg check --approve` decides what to fill), so a deterministic-only or empty graph is a legal, clean state; a project with even one effective LLM rule and no reviewer section is still blocked exactly as before. A cheap upfront check now skips the full per-component scan entirely whenever the project defines no judgment rule at all, so a script-only project's routine `yg check` no longer pays that scan on every run; a project with at least one judgment rule still gets the same accurate, scan-backed decision as before.

### Security

- **A new enforced deterministic rule guards against reviewer credentials ever being sourced from a CLI flag.** Reviewer API keys must come only from the provider's own environment variable (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`) — never a flag, since a flag value lands in shell history and process listings. A new rule (`reviewer-secrets-not-from-flags`) now checks the `init` command and the reviewer-setup modules for two ways that guarantee could be broken: registering a CLI option whose name looks like a credential (`api-key`, `key`, `secret`, `token`, `password`, `credential`), and reading a credential-shaped value off the parsed options object, including destructured or bracket-indexed reads. This is repository-internal enforcement on Yggdrasil's own source — it ships no new surface to adopters.
- **A prose comment mentioning `yg-suppress` can no longer silently waive real violations.** The suppression-marker matcher anchored on a word boundary only, so a comment that merely *talked about* the syntax — e.g. `// never use yg-suppress(*) here because wildcards mask real problems` — was parsed as an active wildcard waiver and silently turned an enforced refusal green; a reasonless prose mention bricked the pair as a check runtime error blamed on the check itself. Markers are now recognized only in anchored position — the token must begin its comment line (after at most one comment delimiter), with a strict aspect-id charset — and one shared matcher now feeds both the reviewer-honoring path and the `yg suppressions` inventory, so the audit lists exactly the waivers that can take effect. Fixed along the way: a marker deep inside a block comment now waives the line it sits on (not the comment's first line), and the inventory no longer shows phantom rows harvested from documentation prose. Behavior note: marker-shaped tokens placed after code on the same line, or mid-sentence inside a comment, are no longer honored — both undocumented forms; the failure direction is stricter enforcement.
- **The portal's local server no longer puts internal error detail in its data responses.** When a background data request to the local portal fails, the server now returns a generic message and writes the full technical reason (paths, stack) to the terminal running the portal — visible only to the person who started it — instead of including it in the response body. Even though the portal only listens on the local loopback address, on a shared machine that address is reachable by other local accounts, so internal detail no longer travels on the wire. The human-readable error page shown in the browser is unchanged.
- **Repository CI and release workflows hardened.** The CI workflow now runs with a least-privilege, read-only token; the release workflow passes the computed version and dist-tag through the environment instead of interpolating them directly into shell commands (injection-safe). These are repository-internal changes with no effect on the published package. (A committed CodeQL workflow briefly added alongside this hardening was removed again before release — code scanning runs via the repository's GitHub setup instead.)

## [5.4.2] - 2026-07-02

### Added

- **The portal shows an instant loading screen and a readable error page.** Opening `yg portal` now paints an immediate loading indicator instead of a blank page while it reads a large graph, then swaps in the full view when it is ready (the address stays the same, so a deep-linked view still opens). If the page cannot be built, the browser now shows a plain-language error page with a concrete next step instead of a raw JSON error blob. Both pages are fully self-contained, so they render even when the portal's own assets are what failed.
- **Four new keyless example projects showing the free, local layer.** `examples/` gains `no-secrets-in-logs` (a deterministic `check.mjs` refusing any log call that references a secret/PII field — a PCI concern), `layered-architecture` (the built-in relation check enforcing `web → domain → data` layering live, no lock, no key), `pure-transforms` (the same deterministic layer over **Python** — reproducible ETL transforms, no wall-clock or randomness), and `checkout-flow` (one deterministic rule attached to a `flow`, propagated to every checkout step). Each is a standalone, runnable project with its own README (scenario → run → the one edit that breaks the rule → the exact refusal), needs **no API key**, and is covered by the shipped-examples e2e guard so they cannot silently rot. Until now every shipped example used the LLM layer; these are the first deterministic + relation examples. The examples index now leads with them.

### Changed

- **Sharper positioning on the README and docs.** The FAQ now answers "how is this different from an agent hook or a pre-commit hook?" head-on — a hook is a real, free, in-loop gate (Yggdrasil's own `check.mjs` is one); the difference is per-file rule delivery, the judgment layer, and a content-addressed lock CI re-proves for free — and the "AI code review bot" answer is refreshed to concede that some bots now run in the loop too (timing alone isn't the difference) and re-anchor on your-specific-rules plus the keyless proof. The README and docs hero/CI cards lead with "a green build can't lie" (each verdict is hash-tied to the exact code and re-proven keyless in CI), and the docs SEO description plus the README hero add the category terms this audience searches — guardrails, policy-as-code for coding agents, drift gate — with a one-line note that "aspect" here is unrelated to aspect-oriented programming. Copy only — no code or behavior change, and no concept was renamed.

### Fixed

- **`yg portal` was broken in the published package — it could not find its own page.** The npm publish allowlist shipped only the compiled JavaScript and grammar assets, so the portal's `shell.html` and stylesheets were never packed; a published install failed on first page load with `ENOENT … shell.html`. The allowlist now ships the portal's `.html` and `.css` assets, and the pack-and-smoke gate asserts the portal assets are present in the tarball so this cannot regress unnoticed.
- **`yg check --approve --only-deterministic` no longer reads as if it reviewed everything.** In deterministic-only mode the run skips the LLM reviewer by design, but the pre-dispatch header and the closing summary previously implied every unverified pair had been handled — the summary even said "all expected pairs hold valid verdicts" while LLM pairs were left unreviewed. Both now count the LLM pairs left unverified and point at a full `yg check --approve` to review them.
- **The `Next:` pointer no longer dead-ends on a bare `Three exits:`.** When the highest-priority next step was a cached refusal — whose guidance is a heading introducing three numbered ways out — the pointer printed only the first line (`Three exits:`) and dropped the options beneath it. A next step whose first line is a heading (ends in a colon) now surfaces the whole block.

## [5.4.1] - 2026-07-01

### Added

- **Non-interactive `yg init` for Docker, devcontainers, and CI.** A fresh graph can now be bootstrapped without the interactive wizard: `yg init --platform <name> --provider <name> --model <m> [--endpoint <url>]`. `--model` is required — init applies no default model. `--endpoint` is defaulted for Ollama and required for an OpenAI-compatible provider; API keys are read from the provider's env var (never a flag, so they never land in shell history). A missing key is non-fatal — the config is written and can be completed later. The interactive wizard's non-TTY error now points at these flags instead of only saying a terminal is required.
- **The reviewer setup now leads with the free, keyless path.** In the interactive wizard the zero-key CLI-agent providers (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI) are listed first and the installed-agent path is pre-selected as the default — most adopters already run one, so the reviewer needs no API key and adds no separate API bill. The getting-started reviewer-setup docs are corrected to match: the model-fetch + connection-validation step applies only to API providers, not the CLI-agent path.
- **Adopter documentation for the portal — a docs-site page and a README preview.** The read-only architecture portal (`yg portal`) now has a dedicated docs page (what it is, how to open the live local view and the self-contained `--static` export, a tour of the views, and the honest-state principle) wired into the site nav under "Author & operate", plus a section with a screenshot in the README so the portal is visible before install. The CLI list in the README also gains a `yg portal` entry. Documentation only — no code or behavior change.

### Fixed

- **CI now passes the portal quality gate it introduced in 5.4.0 (dev-infra only; no change to the published CLI).** The 5.4.0 portal work added a Playwright + Chromium e2e gate and deterministic-cache-backed portal integration tests to `repo-check.sh`, but the CI workflow was never taught to satisfy them in a fresh checkout: Chromium is rebuildable (not committed) and was never installed, and the gitignored deterministic-verdict cache (`.yg-lock.deterministic.json`) was rebuilt only at the very end of `repo-check.sh` — after the tests that read it — so the portal count-parity assertions saw `unverified` pairs and failed. CI now installs Chromium before the gate, `repo-check.sh` rebuilds the deterministic cache before the test run (so a clean clone passes the same as a warm working tree), and the one heavy whole-repo catalogue test carries the same explicit timeout budget as its sibling heavy work so a slow runner no longer trips vitest's 5s default. No source or graph behavior changed.

## [5.4.0] - 2026-06-30

### Added

- **The portal's Rulebook now opens a rule's full detail in the shared inspector panel — including the rule's actual text.** Selecting a rule (in the Rulebook, from a component's effective-aspects list, or via a deep link) now opens it in the same right-hand panel a component opens in — the aspect-side mirror of the component attestation — instead of an inline dropdown under the row. For the first time the panel shows **what the rule actually demands**: the LLM rule's prose (its `content.md`) or, for a rule enforced by a deterministic local check, that check's own source (`check.mjs`); plus its kind / status / scope / `when`, its `includes` (implies) chain (each entry clickable to that rule), and every component it lands on with that component's honest verdict (each cell routing to the component's own panel). The Rulebook list rows now show each rule's plain description (from its `yg-aspect.yaml`) rather than just its name. The inspector panel is wider to fit the rule text, and on a narrow / split-screen window it becomes a right overlay sheet with a close control. The aspect renderer ships as its own focused frontend module so each file stays under the size cap; the data contract gains read-only `description` + `checkSource` fields (the deterministic check source is read from the committed graph — no new engine call, no recomputed count). Covered by updated integration + Playwright/Chromium e2e (the rule detail asserts in the panel, the per-component cell still routes to the component).
- **Portal Playwright + Chromium end-to-end coverage (Phase 5 chunk B) — every §3a surface and transition, proven in a real browser.** The portal now has a real-browser e2e suite (`source/cli/tests/portal-e2e/`, run by Playwright over Chromium) that drives the **real `yg portal` output of real on-disk fixture projects through the public CLI surface only** — it spawns `dist/bin.js` (`yg portal --static` to a `file://` page, or `yg portal --port 0` for a loopback server), opens the page Chromium, and asserts; it **never imports a portal internal and never fabricates a `PortalData`**. Coverage spans every navigable §3a path: each full view **V1–V9** renders its real data plus the always-present honest-state legend (no state collapsed into a single "green"); the **Node Attestation panel** opens from a node reference and shows identity / effective aspects / relations both ways / log / suppressions / a copyable attestation digest; the **⌘K command palette** opens, fuzzy-matches a real entity, and routes; **glossary** tooltips carry plain definitions; the **theme** toggle flips `data-theme`; a **deep link** reopens the entity and its panel on reload; the per-view **transition-table rows** actually navigate (Overview → Start/Coverage/residue, Coverage worklist/rule-header/LIVE-boundary/jump-to-next, Relations hubs/boundary, Rulebook aspect → node cell → panel, Type-Model count/default-rule, Flows card/participant/aspect, Suppressions marker/aspect, Start-here step → Flows / dismiss); **Refresh** re-fetches `/data` read-only and writes nothing; the **Approve** flow shows the dry-run **cost preview** and — confirmed over a temp fixture copy — runs the real deterministic fill so the next data **follows the CLI to green**; **`--no-write`** disables the write control and the server rejects a stray write with **409**; the **file-aware loop** renders a touched file as unverified everywhere (Overview strip, Tree row, panel banner) while the untouched node stays verified; and the **accessibility** contract holds in the real browser (ARIA group + text label on the verdict bar, keyboard-only traversal reaching every native interactive control, the Canvas allowed-relations matrix's DOM-list **mirror**, and `prefers-reduced-motion` collapsing transitions to `0s`). One spec asserts **count parity with `yg check`**: it parses the page's rendered verdict counts and asserts they equal what `yg check` reports on the same fixture (before and after a real Approve), so the portal can never diverge from the CLI. Two new deterministic aspects **lock the discipline in** so a future session cannot regress it: `portal/every-surface-has-e2e` (keyed to a §3a **surface manifest** — the V-stems + the shell panel/refresh/approve/theme/deep-link + the overlays ⌘K/glossary/approve — refuses the suite if any surface loses its covering spec, if a spec omits its `COVERS` marker, if a `COVERS` id is not in the manifest, or — fail-closed — if the manifest cannot be read) and `portal/every-spec-uses-playwright-chromium` (refuses a spec that is not a real Playwright browser test — it must import `@playwright/test` directly or via the project's verified fixture re-export AND actually drive a page, banning a fake/smoke file that asserts nothing in a browser). The suite is wired into `scripts/repo-check.sh` behind a loud Chromium-present guard (a missing browser fails the gate with the install command, never a silent skip), and the spec files are mapped into a new `cli/portal/tests/e2e` test-suite node so the `e2e-public-surface` aspect (no `src/**` import) and the two new aspects all enforce it.
- **Portal audit depth + file-aware loop + accessibility (Phase 5 chunk A).** Three capabilities land on the portal, all read-only and all honest. (1) **Export + attestation digest** — the Coverage & Audit and Suppressions surfaces can now download the coverage summary, the no-rule residue, and the suppression inventory as **CSV / JSON**, built entirely in the page (Blob / data-URI, no network — the offline static export still produces the artifact); and every node's attestation digest is **pinned to the exact committed state it attests** — the node's effective aspects + their verdict states plus the **committed-lock hash and the git commit ref**, both of which now enter `PortalData.meta` through the single backend facade (the lock hash folds only the *committed* lock triad, excluding the gitignored deterministic cache so it is stable across clones; the commit ref is read read-only from `.git` and is honestly `null`, never fabricated, for a non-git directory). (2) **The file-aware loop**, surfaced across every view: a node whose mapped source has changed since its last positive closure now reads **unverified everywhere it appears** — the Overview heartbeat strip, the Structure tree row, and the Node Attestation panel — and the whole-repo cached green can **never** render as a pass over a file you just touched. The freshness signal reuses the engine's own source-fingerprint fold (so it can never diverge from `yg check`) and fires only where there is a committed baseline to compare against, so it adds the one case the pair-state path cannot cover (a node whose green attests specific bytes that have since been edited) without ever painting the repo unverified from a missing baseline. (3) **Accessibility + an org Approve guard** — the verdict bar carries an ARIA group role and a restated-in-text label, every interactive control is a native focusable, keyboard-operable element (the allowed-relations matrix keeps its screen-reader DOM-list mirror), `prefers-reduced-motion` now disables transitions *and* animations, the Approve button shows a free **dry-run cost preview** (reviewer-call / pair budget) and asks for confirmation before the one write, and a `--no-write` view-only / wall-board mode disables the write control in the UI (the loopback server already rejects a stray `POST /approve` with 409). Covered by real-fixture, real-CLI tests (no mocking): the export builders round-trip back to the same rows, the digest pins the commit ref + lock hash, a public-CLI `yg portal --static` before/after a real source edit shows exactly the touched node flip to unverified, and keyboard-traversal / matrix-mirror / view-only-hides-write are asserted over the real committed frontend modules.
- **Portal frontend views — the Rulebook, the Type Model, the Flows lens, the Suppressions inventory, and the Start-here on-ramp.** The portal's catalogue and orientation surfaces (`source/cli/src/templates/portal/js/views/`) are now real, completing the §3a view set — one focused browser module per surface, each consuming the inlined `PortalData` and rendering through the one shared honest-state model so the distinct verdict states are never collapsed into a single "green". The **Rulebook** is the searchable catalogue of every rule the code must satisfy, with kind / status / scope badges, how many units it lands on, and a coverage micro-bar sized by the real verdict states — and the three HONEST tally renderings kept distinct: a normal verified/refused/unverified tally, an aggregating bundle shown as "judges nothing" (it has no own verdict), and a rule-bearing aspect with zero expected units shown as "verifies nothing" with the resolved reason (draft / not effective on any node / scope-and-when excludes every subject); selecting a rule expands the honest per-node cells it lands on, each routing to that node's attestation panel. The **Type Model** presents the architecture vocabulary as capability discovery — every node type with its description, what it nests under, the allowed-relations matrix row (what it may depend on), its default rules, its strict / log flags, and a live node-count — rendering the grammar without inventing any verdict color; a default-rule chip opens the Rulebook and the node-count opens the Structure tree. The **Flows** lens is the business-language bridge: a flow gallery plus a detail view (participants including auto-expanded descendants, each marked and carrying its honest state, plus the flow-level aspects), with the three HONEST flow states kept distinct — a flow whose participants are all unguarded reads `nothing-checked` (never green), any refused/unverified participant makes the whole flow need attention (a weakest-link warning), and only a truly all-verified flow is green. The **Suppressions** inventory is the risk-first ledger of every deliberate waiver — location, the waived rule, the resolved risk flag (wildcard / unbounded / inert-on-draft / typo'd-aspect-id), and the reason — with a standing banner when any marker is wildcard or unbounded, a waived-is-not-a-pass framing on every row (rendered through the shared waived state, never green), and an honest empty state when there are none. The **Start-here** on-ramp is a five-step new-joiner walk assembled live from the committed graph (rot-proof, never hardcoded): what the system is → the big areas of the codebase → one business process end to end → one component explained → reading the colours (the full honest key). The views auto-classify into the existing `enforce: strict` `portal-frontend-view` type, so they inherit and satisfy the honesty aspects (`portal/honest-state-anchor` deterministic + `portal/honest-state-never-collapsed` LLM per-file). They are covered by an integration test that runs the REAL committed view source in a sandbox over the REAL `portal-basic` fixture's data AND the real repo's own `PortalData` produced through the public `yg portal --static` CLI surface — asserting each view renders honestly (aggregating → "judges nothing", vacuous → "verifies nothing" with its reason, an all-no-rule flow → `nothing-checked` and never green, the suppression risk flags surface, the five Start steps derive from the live graph, and no view collapses a non-verified state into green) and that the §3a transitions route through the shared router.
- **Portal frontend views — Overview, Coverage & Audit, the Node Attestation panel, the Structure tree, and Relations & Boundaries.** The portal's per-surface view layer (`source/cli/src/templates/portal/js/views/`) is now real, one focused browser module per surface, each consuming the inlined `PortalData` and rendering through the one shared honest-state model so the distinct verdict states are never collapsed into a single "green". **Overview** opens with a plain-language verdict derived live from the counts (never green while anything is unverified or refused), a "Start here" door to the on-ramp, the residue (no-rule nodes / unmapped files / waivers) turned into clickable links, and a precise-picture preview that opens the full ledger. **Coverage & Audit** draws the 100%-width verdict bar (the verified segment sub-split billed-LLM vs free-deterministic) over the full expected-pair universe with a visible denominator, a hairline-separated non-pair track (no-rule / draft / not-applicable) kept structurally out of the coverage fraction, LIVE-badged counters read from the live data (the boundary-violation total — undeclared plus forbidden-type, never the legitimate declared-only edges — rendered as a real count or `UNKNOWN` when the parse could not run, never a fabricated zero; plus the live blocking-error total that equals `yg check`), a provenance line, the rule-grouped needs-attention worklist in the engine's own priority order, and a jump-to-next that repoints to the residue on an empty worklist rather than dead-ending. The **Node Attestation panel** turns a checkmark into a citable attestation: identity, the effective-aspects table (kind / tier / consensus / cost / status / channel-provenance + the honest verdict state, with the folded input set behind a verified green and the reason behind a refusal), relations in both directions, the when-filtered-out (not-applicable) set as its own list, the per-node log WHY timeline, active suppressions with risk flags, and a copyable attestation digest; a no-rule node links to the Type Model so "nothing here" is never a terminal shrug. The **Structure** view mounts the shared virtualized node tree with honest per-row state. **Relations & Boundaries** renders the allowed-relations matrix on a Canvas grid (with a DOM-list mirror for screen readers), the fan-in / fan-out hub ranking, and the LIVE boundary with its three classes kept honest — `phantom` (undeclared dependency) and `forbiddenType` (a dependency to an architecture-disallowed type) as real violations, `declaredOnly` (a declared relation with no static backing — reflection / DI / HTTP / events) rendered neutrally and never as a violation, and the `UNKNOWN` degraded state surfaced rather than faked clean. Two new honesty aspects lock the layer in: `portal/honest-state-anchor` (deterministic — every view that paints state must reference the shared honest-state model and may never hand-write a green state-class) and `portal/honest-state-never-collapsed` (LLM, per file — the reviewer reads each view against the rule that the distinct states never collapse into green and that declared-only is not a violation), carried by a new `enforce: strict` node type `portal-frontend-view`. The views are covered by an integration test that runs the REAL committed view source in a sandbox over the REAL `portal-basic` fixture's data, asserting each view renders its data, the honest palette is applied (declared-only never marked a violation, no state collapsed, the boundary counter honest including its UNKNOWN form), the live counts equal the pipeline (`== yg check`), and the §3a transitions route through the shared router.
- **Portal frontend foundation — the three-region shell, the honest palette + glossary, the ⌘K command palette, deep-link routing, and the PortalData consumer + tree.** The portal's vanilla browser layer (`source/cli/src/templates/portal/`) is now a real application shell, split into focused single-responsibility modules that each attach to one shared `window.YgPortal` global (a single offline page has no module system, so the serializer inlines the modules in dependency order). The CSS is the full token system — Radix Slate neutrals + Radix Blue accent, light and dark, split into `tokens.css` / `shell.css` / `app.css` — and the **honest status palette** renders every state with color **and** a glyph **and** a border-style **and** a plain-language label, never color alone; an always-visible legend and glossary tooltips say each engine term in plain words. The shell is three regions on every view: a left navigation rail (brand → overview, a ⌘K trigger, grouped view links), a top bar (live status + Refresh/Approve cluster, breadcrumb, ⌘K search, write/view-only indicator, theme toggle), and a right-hand node-panel slot. A vanilla **⌘K command palette** fuzzy-matches every node / aspect / flow plus the view actions and routes the selection; a **URL-hash router** makes every view and entity deep-linkable (view, node, aspect, flow, and a per-verdict `node → aspect → file-unit`, each percent-encoded) so a hash round-trips and reloading reopens exactly what it names, including a node's panel. The **PortalData consumer** reads the inlined snapshot present on both the static export and the served first load, detects served-vs-static, and reaches the read-only Refresh endpoint through an indirect global lookup so the static page provably contains no callable network primitive; the **node tree** is DOM-virtualized (only the visible window of rows is materialized) and laid out with the vendored `d3-hierarchy`, staying responsive on large real graphs. All frontend modules stay framework-free and offline, satisfy the deterministic frontend aspects (no Node imports, no network/CDN, no secret strings, each file focused under the size cap), and are covered by an integration test that evaluates the REAL committed module source in a sandbox over the REAL `portal-basic` fixture's data — asserting the eight honest states each map to a distinct color-class + glyph + label, the router grammar round-trips, the palette routes real entities, the consumer reads the snapshot and detects its delivery mode, and the tree builds the real hierarchy.
- **Additive committed-only configuration read (`parseConfig(..., { skipSecretsOverlay: true })`).** The config parser gained an opt-in mode that skips the local `yg-secrets.yaml` deep-merge overlay entirely, reading only the committed `yg-config.yaml`. The default path is byte-identical to before — when the option is absent, the overlay is loaded and merged exactly as it always was. `loadGraph` / `loadGraphOrAbort` thread a matching `noSecrets` option down to the parser. This is the one additive, non-behavioral core touch that lets a read-only consumer load the graph with a structural guarantee that no developer-local secret file was opened or merged.
- **Portal extraction pipeline scaffold (`source/cli/src/portal/`).** A new read-only extraction core (`extractPortalData`) derives a single typed audit data contract (`PortalData`, in `contract.ts`) by REUSING the existing read-only verification functions — `runCheck` for severities and coverage, `verifyLock` for per-pair states, `computeExpectedPairs` for the pair denominator — never re-implementing a count. The graph is loaded committed-only (`noSecrets`); no lock is written and no reviewer is called. An integration test pins the count-parity gate against the real repository graph: every count the pipeline emits equals what `yg check` reports. The portal subsystem is concreted in the graph itself — two new node types (`portal-contract`, `portal-pipeline`, both `enforce: strict`) carry six deterministic aspects that structurally forbid reading secrets, importing a lock writer, and re-implementing a count, and require the committed-only flag at every loader call.
- **`yg portal --static` now emits a self-contained, offline portal page.** A new backend serializer (`source/cli/src/portal/serializer.ts`) turns one `PortalData` into ONE standalone HTML file: it reads the committed frontend assets (a shell template, the CSS, a vendored layout library, and a bootstrap script), inlines all of them together with the serialized data contract, and writes the result atomically — no network request, no CDN, no runtime npm dependency. The data is embedded inside a JSON `<script>` element and hardened against early element termination (the angle brackets, ampersand, and the two Unicode line/paragraph separators are escaped). The whole-contract serialization seam lives on this covered pipeline node rather than on the command handler, so the portal's structural aspects enforce that boundary. The command (`yg portal --static [--out <path>] [--open]`) extracts the contract committed-only and emits the page, printing its path; `--open` best-effort launches the platform browser without blocking; a non-static invocation surfaces a structured not-yet-available message (the live server lands in a later phase). The frontend ships a minimal-but-real renderer: it parses the inlined data and draws the honest count header (verified / refused / unverified / no-rule / warning, never collapsed) plus a node tree laid out with the vendored `d3-hierarchy` (a compact offline subset — the hierarchy builder + the Reingold–Tilford / Buchheim tidy-tree layout). The frontend is concreted in the graph: two new `enforce: strict` node types (`portal-frontend-core`, `portal-frontend-vendor`) carry deterministic aspects that forbid Node-runtime imports, network/CDN references, and secret strings in browser code, keep each non-vendor file focused, and ban network egress from vendored code (the guard that lets the vendored library be exempt from the file-size cap). A real fixture project (`tests/fixtures/portal-basic`) plus integration and public-surface E2E tests assert the emitted page is offline, carries exactly one inlined-`PortalData` script that round-trips, and is well-formed HTML.
- **`yg portal` (no `--static`) now starts a local read-only loopback server with one explicit write action.** A new server subsystem (`source/cli/src/portal/server/`) binds `node:http` to the loopback interface ONLY (`127.0.0.1` — never `0.0.0.0`, never exposed), serving the live portal page at `/`, a fresh re-extraction of the data contract at `GET /data` (Refresh), the committed frontend assets at `GET /static/*` (path-safe — traversal outside the asset tree is refused), a cost preview at `GET /approve/dry-run`, and the single write at `POST /approve`. **Refresh is read-only:** every `/data` call re-runs the extraction from scratch and persists NOTHING — no lock write, no deterministic-cache write, no reviewer call — so the committed lock and the gitignored deterministic cache are byte-unchanged across any number of refreshes (asserted by an integration test). The meta block surfaces the configured `auto_approve` mode and a freshness stamp. **Approve is the one write, and it shells out:** `POST /approve {llm}` spawns the existing CLI as a child process (`yg check --approve`, plus `--only-deterministic` when the LLM checkbox is off) — the server never re-implements the fill, never imports a lock writer, and never reads secrets (the spawned CLI owns keys and the lock write); the next `/data` reflects any new refusal, never a silent success. A view-only mode (`--no-write`) disables the write entirely, rejecting `POST /approve` with HTTP 409, for shared / wall-board deployments. The server is concreted in the graph: a new `enforce: strict` node type `portal-server` carries deterministic aspects that pin the loopback bind (`portal/loopback-only` — every `listen()` must specify a loopback host), pin the Approve shape (`portal/approve-shells-cli-only` — a literal `--approve` flag, a constant bin reference, no env impersonation, and no in-process fill call), and forbid reading secrets or importing a lock writer; the server reaches the engine ONLY through the portal's own pipeline and serializer — it declares no relation to any engine subsystem. Real in-process integration tests and a public-surface E2E (spawning `yg portal --port 0`) drive every endpoint against a real fixture project, including a genuine deterministic Approve on a temp copy.
- **Portal data contract is now fully populated — per-node honest state, catalogues, hubs, residue, and a rule-grouped worklist.** Three new derivation modules extend the pipeline (all reusing the engine's own read-only results, never recomputing): `derive-nodes.ts` builds one entry per graph node carrying `checked` (has a non-draft, non-aggregate effective aspect), the effective-aspect rows (kind / tier / consensus / cost / status / channel / origin + the per-pair verdict state), the node's own honest state (worst pair state, else `no-rule` when unchecked, else `warning` for a node-scoped advisory) kept SEPARATE from a bottom-up roll-up over descendants, both relation directions, the parsed log, and per-node suppressions; `derive-catalogue.ts` builds the aspect catalogue (kind / status / scope / when / implies / rule prose) with three HONEST tally renderings — normal verified/refused/unverified, an aggregating rule that "judges nothing", and a rule-bearing aspect resolving to zero subjects that "verifies nothing" with a resolved reason — plus the flow catalogue (participants auto-expanded with descendants, flow aspects, and an honest flow state that is `nothing-checked` rather than green when no participant carries a rule) and the architecture type model (description / parents / allowed-relations matrix / default aspects / strict + log flags / live node count); `derive-rest.ts` builds the fan-in/out hub ranking, the no-rule + uncovered residue ledger, and the worklist by REUSING the CLI's own issue grouping (`groupIssues`) so its rule grouping and priority cascade are identical to `yg check`. (The live dependency-boundary and suppression inventory that this entry once left as UNKNOWN / empty are now fully populated — see the portal-facade refactor under "Changed".)
- Architecture relations now support a per-type `default: allow | deny` policy, a `['*']` wildcard target (any type), and a meaningful empty list — enabling pure sinks and per-relation-type lock-downs in `yg-architecture.yaml`.

### Fixed

- **Six portal layout bugs that made the page hard to read and navigate are fixed; the chrome now stays put and content uses the space.** (1) **The detail panel no longer clips long content.** The right-hand node panel had hidden overflow, so a long refusal reason or the bytes-attested drill-through was cut off; it is now its own full-height scroll column, so long content scrolls inside the panel instead of disappearing. The center stage's narrow width cap was also raised and centered, so wide surfaces (the relations matrix, the structure tree, tables) use the available width while long prose stays readable. (2) **The left navigation rail now stays pinned.** It previously scrolled away with the page; it now sticks in place with its own scroll, so the tabs are always reachable. (3) **The top bar now stays pinned.** It previously scrolled away; it now sticks to the top over a solid background, above the content. (4) **A spurious horizontal scrollbar is gone.** The closed detail-panel slot drew a one-pixel border that pushed the document one pixel past the viewport on every view; the closed slot now draws no border, so there is no horizontal scrollbar (the relations matrix also scrolls within its own container rather than pushing the page). (5) **A selected rule's detail now opens beside the rule, not below the whole list.** Clicking a rule used to render its detail under the entire catalogue, pushing it far down the page; the detail now opens inline directly under the clicked row (accordion-style), keeping the per-rule tally and the honest per-node cells visible right where you clicked. (6) **The colour key is now one pinned, collapsible bar at the bottom — not repeated inside every view.** The full key was appended to the bottom of every view's scrolling content, a long block that could not be pinned; it is now a single compact row of state chips pinned to the bottom of the window, expandable on click to the full descriptions. Every state stays distinct (colour + glyph + label) and the honesty model is unchanged — "verified" is still the only green and no state is collapsed away.
- **The portal no longer reports an advisory refusal as a blocking "refused" — its headline now matches `yg check` exactly.** A rule whose status is **advisory** is non-blocking signal: when it is refused, the honesty model renders it as a **warning**, never as a blocking "refused" (this is how `yg check` reports it). The portal was instead reading the **raw** verdict, so an advisory refusal showed up as a blocking refusal everywhere — it inflated the "refused" headline count, painted its owning component red, AND was double-displayed (it was already counted under warnings) — even though `yg check` reported no blocking failures. The portal now adjusts every **displayed** verdict state by the rule's effective status at one shared seam before it reaches any count, component pill, per-rule row, or per-rule tally: a refusal on an **advisory** rule displays as a warning; a refusal on an **enforced** rule stays a blocking refusal; the verified and not-yet-confirmed states are unchanged. The blocking-refusal count is therefore **enforced refusals only** — exactly what blocks a build — and advisory refusals move into their own non-blocking bucket so the coverage ledger still accounts for every expected check without ever painting non-blocking signal as a blocking failure. The advisory refusal stays fully visible and still carries the reviewer's stated reason; it is simply shown as the non-blocking warning it is. The count-parity gate (the portal's numbers can never diverge from `yg check`) is preserved and tightened: the integration test now asserts, on the real repository, that the blocking-refusal count is zero while the advisory refusal is reflected as a warning, and that every expected check is still accounted for.
- **The portal's Structure view now has an in-tree filter, and selecting a component reveals it in the hierarchy.** At several hundred components, finding where one lives in the tree had no direct affordance — the only search was the command palette, which jumped to a component's panel without showing its place in the hierarchy. The Structure view now has a filter box that prunes the tree to matching components plus their ancestors as you type, and selecting a component from the palette (or opening a deep-link to it) scrolls the tree to that row and marks it, so you land on the component in its hierarchy, not only in the side panel.
- **The portal's node panel explains its reviewer vocabulary in plain language on hover.** The per-component attestation panel showed dense internal terms — the reviewer kind and tier, the vote count, whether a check is billed or free, and where a rule attaches from (set directly / inherited / by type / from a flow or a consumed contract) — with no explanation for a non-expert. Each now carries a plain-language tooltip drawn from the same glossary the rest of the page already uses, and the panel gained a close control (needed when it appears as an overlay on a narrow window).
- **The portal stays usable in a narrow or split-screen window.** Below ~900px the fixed side panel and top bar forced a horizontal scrollbar, clipped the page heading, and — with a component panel open — let the top-bar status text overlap the panel title. A narrow-width layout now turns the side panel into a right overlay sheet (with a close control) instead of a reserved column, and collapses the top bar's verbose caption, so no view forces horizontal scrolling or overlaps the header. (A phone-width layout remains out of scope for this desktop tool.)
- **Several small muted-grey portal labels now meet WCAG AA contrast, in both themes.** One too-light grey token was used for body-size text that fell below the AA 4.5:1 threshold — the nav section labels, the load-bearing "Absence of red is not a pass…" caption, the structure-tree type labels, the rulebook per-cell state words, and the draft / not-applicable state glyphs. The token is darkened in the light theme and lightened in the dark theme (one value per theme) so every element that resolves through it clears AA at once (measured ~5:1 in both themes, up from ~3:1), while staying a step lighter than the secondary-text token so the muted/secondary hierarchy is preserved.
- **The portal's nav-rail brand mark is larger and properly seated.** The world-tree logo chip was small enough (26px) that the mark's fine detail muddied, and in the light theme the deep chip sat on the near-white rail as a hard-edged tile that looked pasted on. The chip is now 36px (the tree reads clearly) with a soft drop shadow and hairline that seat it on the light chrome while staying unobtrusive in the dark theme. (The 16px browser-tab favicon, where this detailed mark necessarily smears, is unchanged — a clean small-size icon would need a simplified variant of the brand asset.)
- **The portal's node panel now states the reason an advisory rule flagged a component, instead of a bare warning glyph.** On the per-node attestation panel an advisory (non-blocking) refusal rendered only its warning glyph with no explanation — the reason text was shown in the Coverage and Rulebook views but not on the panel where a human inspects that one component. The panel now shows the reviewer's reason for an advisory flag (as it already did for a blocking refusal), followed by an explicit "Advisory rule — non-blocking signal, not a pass and not a clean check" line, so a warning reads honestly on its own on the product's primary attestation surface.
- **A read-only portal export no longer presents an enabled "Approve" button it cannot honour.** In a static export (a self-contained file with no server) the primary Approve button shipped enabled and only revealed it was inert *after* a click, and Refresh was disabled with no explanation — both eroded the honest read-only promise. Approve is now disabled up front in any non-served page (it was already correct in view-only served mode), and both Approve and Refresh carry a plain reason ("Available only when the portal is served by yg portal — this is a read-only export") so an assistive-tech or keyboard user sees why. The nav-rail tag and live pill now read "snapshot" / "Exported snapshot" on a static file rather than "live view," so the page never labels a frozen export as live.
- **A stale or mistyped portal deep-link now says so, instead of silently redirecting.** An unknown view hash (e.g. a renamed-away view) silently fell back to Overview, and a deep-link to a removed flow silently showed a *different* flow as if the link had resolved — both reading as a successful navigation. The page now shows an honest "Nothing at '<x>' — showing Overview." banner for an unknown view, and "No flow named '<x>' — it may have been removed." for an unknown flow (entity links to a removed node or rule already said so). Matches the product's honest-state ethos that a navigation never appears to succeed when it did not.
- **A risky-waiver flag in the portal's Suppressions view no longer borrows the colour reserved for a failed verdict.** The "WILDCARD — all aspects off" risk label was painted in the same saturated red the portal reserves for the "refused" verdict state, so a waiver *risk* could read as a failed check. It is now amber (the warning channel), matching the sibling unbounded-range flag, preserving the rule that saturated red means a refused verdict only. The honest "suppressed" verdict badge was always shown separately and was never masked; this is palette-consistency only.
- **The portal's per-rule attestation caveat about reviewer-tier config now appears only on rules an LLM judges, not on deterministic checks.** The drill-through under a verified rule appended a note that the reviewer's tier may be locally overridden via a secrets overlay (only the tier name is hashed, shown values come from committed config) — accurate for an LLM-judged rule, but meaningless and misleading on a deterministic check, which has no reviewer and no tier. The note is now gated to LLM rules; a deterministic check shows its bytes-attested drill-through without it.
- **A graph-aware deterministic check that refuses because it cannot reach a target node now re-verifies itself once the reaching relation is declared.** When a check probed another node and treated an out-of-reach probe as a violation, the refusal was cached but the probe was never folded into the verdict's inputs — so declaring the relation that makes the target reachable left the stale refusal in place (`yg check` stayed red over code the check would now pass). The unreachable probe now folds the calling node's graph observation, so adding the relation invalidates the cached verdict and the next `yg check --approve` re-verifies it green — with no manual cache deletion. The content-addressed verdict format (and its golden tests) is unchanged; only which observations a relation-reading check folds was corrected.
- **`yg aspect-test` on a deterministic aspect whose check errors now shows the real, structured cause instead of a generic "please file an issue".** A check that throws, returns the wrong shape, is async, or reports a violation against a file it was not given is an actionable aspect-author error — but the node-scoped path (including under `--check-determinism`) routed it through the generic unclassified-error wrapper and leaked an internal code prefix. It now renders the same what/why/next message the ad-hoc file path already did, with a clean non-zero exit.
- **Removed a stray NUL byte from a CLI source file so its diffs render as text, not binary.** A control character embedded in a deduplication key made the file register as binary data to `git` and `file`, hiding all of its diffs; the separator is now plain text with identical de-duplication behavior.
- **The portal's four parked honesty guarantees are now genuinely enforced, not declared.** A final audit had left four portal items as fail-safe/limits; each is now closed and re-probed (an evasion fires, the real case stays silent). (1) **Node-attestation drill-through now carries data.** The per-node attestation panel promises that a green cites the exact bytes it attests and a refused verdict cites the reviewer's reason — but the backend derivation never populated those fields, so both blocks always rendered empty. Each effective-aspect row now carries `foldedInputs` (the verdict's covered subject files) on a verified row and `reason` (the refusal message) on a refused row; on the real repo every verified row now cites its files and the one refused pair cites its reason. (2) **Surface-coverage now binds a claim to real navigation.** The `every-surface-has-e2e` check verified only that a spec *declared* it covers a surface (its `COVERS` list); a spec could claim every surface while its body never opened any. Each surface now maps to a navigation marker (its hash route, key chord, or load-bearing DOM seam) and a spec that claims a surface its body never drives is refused — so a coverage claim can no longer outrun the test. (3) **The honest-state anchor now catches an ad-hoc fabricated green.** The `honest-state-anchor` tripwire demanded the shared honest-state model only when a view touched a `state-*` class or a state-helper name; a view that painted its own green through an unrelated class (e.g. `'is-green ok-cell'`) slipped through. The trigger now also fires on a green/pass color class token (matched per CSS class token, so a prose sentence or a glossary key is never a false positive), so a hand-painted green still requires the shared anchor. (4) **The offline proof now scans only the executable surface.** The static-page offline assertion greped the whole emitted document for `fetch(` / URLs / CDN hosts — but the inlined JSON data legitimately contains those strings (node descriptions, source snippets), so the proof was at risk of false-positiving on real data (on the real repo it does). It now parses out the inlined `application/json` data region first and asserts no executable network reference in the remainder, with a strict-guard test proving a genuine `<script src=http>` / `fetch('http…')` on the code surface still fails.
- **The relation-conformance dependency extractor now detects no-substitution template-literal `import()` / `require()` specifiers.** A dependency written as `` import(`./x.js`) `` or `` require(`./x.js`) `` (a backtick literal with no `${…}`) is static and statically resolvable — TypeScript, esbuild, and Node treat it identically to a quoted string — but the TS/JS extractor only accepted a plain `string` AST node, so such an edge was silently missed: a real undeclared cross-node dependency written with backticks produced no `relation-undeclared-dependency` error. The dynamic-import/require branch now reads the specifier through a helper that also accepts a no-substitution `template_string` (an interpolated template literal stays non-static and is correctly skipped). No repository code used the form, so the change surfaces no new errors — it closes the false-negative for the future.
- **Grouped `yg check` output now surfaces EACH affected node's own fix when the fix is node-specific.** Previously a group collapsed same-rule issues and showed only the alphabetically-first member's `Fix:`/`Why:` — for codes whose remedy names the node (a missing per-node log entry, declaring a dependency in one node's file, architecture relation errors whose rationale differs per node), the rendered single command pointed at one node only, so an agent would fix it, re-run, and stay red. The block now prints each node's own command (and divergent rationale) when members diverge; genuinely shared fixes (reviewer refusals, unverified pairs) still collapse to one line.
