- **`yg check --approve --dry-run --quiet` no longer silently drops the budget preview.** The write sink made `--quiet` swallow the dry-run cost breakdown — the command's primary deliverable — leaving empty output. `--dry-run` now wins over `--quiet`: the budget always reaches stdout; `--quiet` only suppresses the non-dry-run fill progress on stderr.
- **`yg check --aspect <unknown-id>` now errors clearly instead of a misleading `0 of N errors` FAIL.** An unrecognized or mistyped aspect id produced a zero-count drill-in that looked like the rule simply had no issues this run. It is now a guided error naming the unknown id (with `yg aspects` as the next step). Relatedly, when a valid aspect has no issues this run while other errors remain, the drill-in now falls through to the global `Next:` instead of dead-ending with no next step.
- **`yg check --summary` / `--top` (and `--details` / `--aspect`) combined with `--only-deterministic` are now rejected.** The read-only triage views silently dropped the requested deterministic fill (the view override forced read-only), so the user believed they had filled the deterministic pairs when they had not. The contradictory combination now produces a guided error and a non-zero exit, mirroring the existing flag-mutex rejections.
- **The AST fact cache no longer fails closed-to-empty for a C# file with a malformed shard.** A cached shard that matched a C# file's content key but was missing its C#-specific extract was treated as a valid "no C# data" hit, silently skipping that file downstream — erasing a real cross-node C# dependency and letting the relation gate go falsely green. Such a shard is now treated as a cache miss, so the file is re-parsed (fail-closed-to-parse, never to empty). Non-C# files are unaffected.
- **Widened the AST fact-cache key from 64 to 128 bits** so a birthday collision (which could serve one file's cached extract for another and slip past the cache's identity check) is infeasible. Existing shards keyed at the old width are ignored and re-parsed once; the cache is gitignored and rebuildable, so no action is required.

### Changed

- **The portal now carries the Yggdrasil name and brand mark — the "Heartwood" codename is gone from the page, and the world-tree logo is the tab icon and the nav-rail mark.** The browser tab reads "Yggdrasil Portal" and shows the logo as its favicon; the nav-rail brand pairs the logo (on a deep chip so the gold mark reads on either theme) with the name "Yggdrasil"; and the page heading is now just the project's own name (the product brand having moved to the rail), so a repo named anything else reads cleanly without a redundant suffix. The logo ships fully inline as a `data:image/svg+xml` URI built once by the serializer from a committed brand-asset module, so the page stays one self-contained offline file with no network or file reference for the image; the nav-rail brand reuses that same inline URI. Static export and the served page remain byte-identical. The portal e2e gate's Playwright config joins the existing test/build tooling configs (ESLint, Vitest, tsup) the `ci-config` type classifies, and is mapped onto the existing `cli/config/linters` node — so the new config file is graph-covered like every other runner config rather than reading as an unmapped, off-graph file.
- **The portal backend now reaches the engine through ONE read-only facade, and carries the FULL live dependency boundary plus the live suppression inventory.** The extraction pipeline previously declared direct relations into ~a dozen engine subsystems (graph load, check, lock verification, pair computation, the relation analyzer, suppression scan, issue grouping, the effective-aspect cascade, reviewer-tier selection, the log reader). That spider is collapsed into a single seam: a new module `source/cli/src/portal/engine-api.ts` (with `portal/api/boundary.ts` and `portal/api/suppress-scan.ts`) is the portal's SOLE gateway to the engine — it wraps every read-only call the portal needs, imports no lock writer, and loads the graph committed-only (`noSecrets`). A new `enforce: strict` node type `portal-engine-api` carries the read-only portal aspects (no-secrets-import, no-lock-writer-import, committed-only-flag, focused-file-size) plus the count-parity manifest, so the single seam is the one place the engine-reuse and read-only guarantees are enforced. The pipeline (`extract.ts` + `derive-*.ts`) now imports ONLY the facade and the data contract — its relation matrix is just `calls: [portal-engine-api]` / `uses: [portal-contract]` — and the boundary/suppression seam types moved into the contract so both producer and consumer share one home. Using that facade, the live boundary is now FULLY computed (previously honestly UNKNOWN): **phantom** (a real code dependency on a mapped node with no declared relation, from the relation pass), **declared-only** (a declared structural relation with no static code backing — DI / HTTP / events — a graph-MINUS-detected join), and **forbidden-type** (a detected dependency whose target type the architecture matrix forbids — a detected-edges × matrix join); it is `unknown` only when the relation parse genuinely throws, never fabricated-clean. The live suppression inventory is likewise populated, reusing the EXACT scan `yg suppressions` runs (the scan implementation moved behind the facade and `yg suppressions` imports it from there — its output is unchanged), with each marker tagged by resolved risk (wildcard / typo / inert-draft / unbounded).
- **Additive read-only detected-edge accessor on the relation pass (`RelationPassResult.detectedEdgesByNode`).** `runRelationPass` now also returns the FULL set of uniquely-resolved cross-node code edges per source node (the superset of the undeclared-violations it already returns), excluding self / ancestor / descendant edges. It is populated from the per-node resolution the verdict pass already computes, so it adds no parse and changes NO verdict logic — purely additive. The portal boundary's declared-only and forbidden-type classes are pure joins over this set; a unit test pins the superset semantics (a declared edge appears in the detected set but not in the violations).
- **Hardened the portal FRONTEND and e2e-enforcing deterministic aspects against evasions and false positives, and closed a map-vs-manifest coverage gap.** A final adversarial pass over the guards that protect the portal's offline + honest properties found, and this change closes, four issues — each re-probed so the evasion now fires and the legitimate case stays silent. The offline guard (`portal/no-cdn-no-network`) now catches a bare protocol-relative `//host` reference reached through indirection in quoted/executable context (a `'//evil.example.com/x.js'` assigned to a variable then handed to `setAttribute('src', …)`) and a CSS `@import "//host/…"` — previously silent, breaking the offline guarantee — while no longer firing on an `http(s)://` URL inside a JavaScript COMMENT (a license/attribution/doc link), which it now skips via the file's AST comment ranges exactly like the sibling no-network-egress guard, so a real `<script src=http>` / `url(http)` is still caught but a doc link no longer blocks the build. The vendored-code egress guard (`portal/no-network-egress`) now detects image/audio beacon exfil — `new Image()` / `new Audio()` whose `.src` is assigned a URL, inline or via a variable (the classic cookie-exfil channel that needs no fetch) — with its description made honest that a fully-computed callee name (`window[['f','e','t','c','h'].join('')]`) is a known deterministic limit the live offline e2e is the real proof for. The §3a surface manifest was missing `shell-nav` (the persistent navigation rail) and `shell-prov` (the provenance pins), so `portal/every-surface-has-e2e` could never enforce them; both are added to `SURFACE_MANIFEST` and now covered by real specs that actually drive them (the views suite clicks and asserts the nav rail; the panel suite asserts the commit-ref + lock-hash provenance pins). The descriptions of the two e2e-enforcing tripwires were made honest about what a green means: `every-surface-has-e2e` states that `COVERS` is a DECLARED surface→spec mapping (no holes) and the real proof each surface is driven is the live `npm run test:e2e:portal` Chromium run plus the `e2e-public-surface` black-box aspect; `honest-state-anchor` states it is a structural tripwire that demands the shared-states anchor only when a file touches the honest-state vocabulary, with the per-file LLM aspect `honest-state-never-collapsed` as the primary semantic guard.
- **Hardened the six portal Phase-1 deterministic aspects against AST-level evasions, and pinned the count-parity gate's bucketing with synthetic coverage.** An adversarial pass found real escapes in the portal's structural guards; each is now closed and re-probed: the no-secrets-import guard also catches a direct filesystem read of `yg-secrets.yaml` (`readFileSync`/`readFile`/`createReadStream`/`openSync` and their `fs/promises` forms, string or template-literal path) and a dynamic `import('…secrets-parser…')` plus the namespace member calls that follow it; the committed-only-flag guard resolves local aliases of the loader symbols (`import { loadGraph as load }`, `const lg = loadGraph`) before checking for the safe flag, so an aliased call can no longer skip it; the no-lock-writer guard treats a member READ of a writer off a lock-store namespace alias (`const persist = store.writeLock`) as a violation, not only a direct call; the count-parity guard tightens its raw-`.verdicts` receiver match (member and subscript forms) and also catches a hand-rolled loop/reducer over a local bound from `lock.verdicts` (`const entries = lock.verdicts`, `const { verdicts } = lock`), with its description made honest that the negative arm is a tripwire while the positive node-id manifest plus the parity test are the real guarantee; and the focused-file-exports heuristic now counts each runtime binding (per declarator in `export const a=1,b=2`, per non-type specifier in `export { a, b, c }`) rather than one per export statement, still excluding type-only exports. `buildCounts` is now exported so a new synthetic-pairs test drives every `PairState` kind and asserts the bucketing identity (verified + refused + unverified equals the full expected-pair set) and that the two fail-closed gate states (`prompt-too-large`, `companion-error`) land in unverified — never green, never dropped.
- **The relation-conformance pass now caches per-file AST extraction, so an unchanged file is no longer re-parsed every `yg check`.** A new content-addressed per-file fact cache lives at `.yggdrasil/.ast-cache/` (gitignored, rebuildable, never committed): each file's pure extractor output (declarations + detected uses, and for C# the alias-unresolved pre-assembly extract) is keyed by the file's raw content hash + language + grammar wasm hash + extractor revision. A hit skips the tree-sitter parse entirely while the symbol-table rebuild and the whole resolve/verify join stay LIVE every run, so the relation verdict is still the current truth of the code against the graph — a cached fact can never carry a stale relation verdict, and a failed parse is never cached (it re-parses every run, fail-closed-to-parse). C# alias maps are (de)serialized as entry arrays at the cache boundary so a cross-file `global using` alias edge still resolves on a cached run. `yg init --upgrade` adds `.ast-cache/` to the local `.yggdrasil/.gitignore`.
- **Retired the `.symbols-cache` per-language symbol-index persistence layer.** The `loadSymbolIndex`, `writeSymbolIndex`, `PersistedSymbolIndex`, and `SYMBOL_INDEX_VERSION` exports have been removed from `symbol-table.ts`; the `relationIndexDir` helper and `index-dir.ts` module have been deleted. The `SymbolTable` class (the in-memory structure built on every run) is unchanged. The `.symbols-cache/` entry in `.yggdrasil/.gitignore` remains harmless for existing projects.
- **E2E test suites now drive only the public CLI surface — no imports from `src/`, in any form.** Every test under `source/cli/tests/e2e/` previously reached into CLI internals: statically (`readLock` and the lock-path/name constants from `io/lock-store`, the `LockFile` type and `LOCK_NONDET_FILE_NAME` from `model/lock`, the init/config/api-key writers in the API-key-secrets suite) AND dynamically (11 `await import('templates/platform.js')` calls plus an `await import('core/graph-loader.js')` for `CLI_SUPPORTED_SCHEMA` in the lifecycle suite). All such imports are removed. Lock-state assertions now read the committed lock triad (`yg-lock.nondeterministic.json` + `yg-lock.logs.json` + the gitignored `.yg-lock.deterministic.json`) directly via a new self-contained test helper (`tests/e2e/support/read-lock.ts`) that merges them into the same `{ version, verdicts, nodes }` view; the API-key-secrets suite hand-authors the documented `reviewer.tiers.<tier>.config.api_key` overlay and proves a real `yg check` accepts it end-to-end; the platform-install tests now drive `yg init --upgrade --platform <x>` as a black box and assert the installed rules file(s) on disk; and the version-advance test asserts only the observable migration effect (version advanced off the seed, valid semver, legacy `schemas/` removed, idempotent re-run) with the exact-equality-to-`CLI_SUPPORTED_SCHEMA` check moved into the migrations unit suite. The dead e2e→src graph relations were dropped accordingly. A focused `parseConfig` unit test pins the tier `api_key`/`endpoint` branches the old in-process import had covered incidentally.
- **New deterministic aspect `e2e-public-surface` enforces the black-box contract structurally.** Attached to the `cli/tests/e2e` parent node (cascading to all e2e child nodes, and reaching no unit-test node), it parses each e2e test file with tree-sitter and refuses any file that imports a module resolving into `source/cli/src/**` — in any form (`import … from`, `import type … from`, `export … from`, dynamic `import()`, `require()`, a no-substitution template-literal `` import(`…`) ``/`` require(`…`) ``, or TS `import x = require()`). Detection is AST-based (import/export `source` nodes and the string argument of dynamic-import / require call expressions), so a string literal that merely contains the word `import` (e.g. a fixture written via `writeFileSync` to seed an undeclared dependency) is not a false positive, and `support/**` helpers that import nothing from `src/**` pass. A future session can no longer reintroduce internal coupling in the e2e suite without `yg check` refusing it.
- An explicit empty relation target list (e.g. `uses: []`) in `yg-architecture.yaml` now means "deny all targets for that relation type". Previously it was silently dropped and behaved as allow-all. Graphs that relied on `[]` being ignored are affected; omitting the key preserves the old allow-all behavior.

## [5.3.0] - 2026-06-26

### Added

- **`yg impact --file` now reports one precise reviewer-call total.** It replaces the owner-scoped one-line cost and the symbolic `reviewer requests × consensus × units` formula with a single `Total to re-verify:` block — billed reviewer calls / free deterministic pairs / currently-green verdicts re-rolled — preceded by a per-node breakdown tagged with why each node is affected (`own pairs` / `references this file` / `companion observes this file` / `deterministic check observes this file`). For a cold companion-backed LLM aspect (no lock entry yet) it runs the companion **resolver** — gated by a static allowed-reads pre-filter and a timeout — to compute the exact dependency set, so the estimate is precise before the first fill; it makes no LLM call, never runs `check.mjs`, and writes nothing. A companion whose hook fails to resolve is listed under `Unresolved` (cost unknown; it will infra-fail at fill) rather than silently counted as zero. Editing a graph file under `.yggdrasil/` prints a redirect to `yg impact --aspect <id>` instead of a misleading per-pair total. Fixes a bug where the cost preview under-reported a companion-backed blast radius (the real cross-node cost was hidden behind a formula while a prominent owner-scoped `0 LLM` line read like a grand total).
- **Companion-authoring guidance: a companion's verdict folds in everything the hook reads to decide.** The agent rules, knowledge topics, and the aspect schema now state that what a companion reads to *decide* (not only the paths it *returns*) folds into every pair's verdict — so editing any read-to-decide file re-bills its readers. Read the single needed file via `ctx.fs.read` rather than materializing a whole related node via `ctx.graph.node(...)`, which reads that node's entire content into every per-file pair and breaks per-unit isolation.
- **`yg check` output is now grouped by rule (the headline redesign).** Issues are grouped by rule instead of one block per `(rule × file)`: the shared Why and Fix renders once per group, and affected nodes are listed beneath it. `unverified` collapses into a single group regardless of how many rules are unverified — the aspect id appears per body line (`- <node>  aspect '<id>'`) rather than in the header. `refused`, `prompt-too-large`, and `aspect-companion-runtime-error` remain per-aspect groups; each refused or relation-error node shows its full per-node detail (reviewer reason or violation file:lines) on its body line. The section subheader reads `Errors (N) in M groups:` with the true total; when the group count is large, the output is capped with a pointer to the triage flags below. The `Next:` line is annotated when the suggested command only partially clears the errors (e.g. `--approve` fills unverified pairs but refused or relation errors remain). Exported `CODE_ONLY_GROUP_CODES` from `group-issues.ts` for use in tests and tooling.
- **New read-only triage flags for large check walls.** `--details` prints the ungrouped, one-block-per-issue format (the previous default). `--aspect <id>` drills into a single rule's group with full per-node detail. `--top [N]` now shows the N highest-priority groups (not individual issues); a bare `--top` keeps only the single suggested-next line. `--summary` (per-node counts) is unchanged. Every narrowed view always prints the true aggregate `Errors (N)` / `Warnings (N)` header and preserves the real exit code, so a narrowed view can never read as a clean build.
- **`--approve` progress moved to stderr with a scale-aware heartbeat.** A large or slow fill no longer looks hung: approvals are summarized, refusals and infrastructure failures surface immediately. On a TTY a live-updating line shows progress; on a non-TTY periodic milestones and a "still working" line are emitted. Repeated infrastructure diagnostics are grouped by root cause. Stdout stays the clean final report. New `--quiet` / `-q` flag silences the progress stream entirely.
- **`--no-approve` flag** forces a read-only check even when `auto_approve` is configured.
- **`auto_approve` config option** (`false` | `deterministic` | `full`, default `false`): bare `yg check` auto-fills without a flag. `deterministic` behaves like `--approve --only-deterministic` (free, keyless); `full` behaves like `--approve`. Explicit CLI flags always override the config. A PASS produced by a config-driven auto-fill is marked `(auto-filled)`; `full` prints a one-line banner before calling the reviewer.
- **Emoji decoration on color-capable terminals.** The verdict line is prefixed with `✅` (PASS) or `❌` (FAIL); the `Errors (N):` subheader is prefixed with `❌` and `Warnings (N):` with `⚠️`. Decoration only — verdict and severity text are always present. When color is disabled (`NO_COLOR`, non-TTY, or `chalk.level === 0`) the output is byte-identical to the text-only format: no emoji, no extra space. CI logs and screen-reader output are unaffected.

### Changed

- **Group body lines now include `what` detail for structural and graph-validation issues.** Body lines previously showed only `- <node>`. Structural errors — `when-predicate-invalid`, `log-entry-missing`, `mapping-path-missing`, `flow-node-broken`, `aspect-undefined`, `port-undefined`, and similar — carry a specific per-instance diagnostic that now appears on the body line (`- <node>  <what summary>`), so the agent can see the specific reason without running a separate command.

## [5.2.6] - 2026-06-25

### Fixed

- **Four additional WASM tree lifetime bugs fixed; `withParsedFile` introduced as the canonical lifecycle-safe parse API.** `parseFile()` returns a `Tree` backed by the WASM heap — the JS GC cannot reclaim it and `.delete()` must be called explicitly. Three call sites had incorrect or missing cleanup: (1) `suppressions.ts scanMarkersForFile` called `tree.delete()` on the happy path only — if `scanSuppressionMarkersInComments` threw, the catch block returned the fallback without deleting the tree; (2) `suppress-ranges.ts resolveSuppressedRangesForPrompt` called `tree?.delete()` after `collectSuppressions` but outside any `finally`, so a throw there also leaked; (3) `structure/hook-loader.ts runCompanionHook` allocated a fresh `ParseCache` when no external cache was provided but never called `destroyParseCache` in a finally — every tree warmed by a companion hook run leaked permanently. A fourth `ast/runner.ts` bug: a `Tree` created by `parseFile()` was pushed to `localTrees` (cleaned up by the outer finally) only after a `hasError` check that could throw `AstRunnerError` first — if that throw fired, the tree was not yet in `localTrees` and was never freed. All four sites are now fixed. The root cause is that `parseFile` exposes a raw WASM resource where correct use requires discipline that the type system cannot enforce. Added `withParsedFile(path, content, fn)` to `ast/parser.ts` — it parses, calls `fn(tree)`, and guarantees `tree.delete()` in a finally block; `parseFile` direct import is now guarded by the new `wasm-tree-lifecycle` deterministic aspect (see below). Updated `docs/getting-started.md` to reflect that coverage scanning is now disk-based, not git-tracked-file-based.
- **`yg check --approve --only-deterministic` no longer crashes with `Aborted()` on large repos (complete fix).** The 5.2.5 fix freed `Parser` and `Tree` objects that accumulated across pairs, but the `Aborted()` crash persisted because the relation-conformance pass (which always runs, even with zero unverified pairs) accumulated all per-file parse trees simultaneously in a shared cache — on a large repo, hundreds of trees lived concurrently in the WASM heap and exhausted it mid-loop, before the end-of-function cleanup could run. The pass now uses a parse-and-immediately-free discipline: each file is parsed inline and its tree deleted with `try/finally` immediately after the extractor call, so at most one tree is live at any moment. On first run, symbol-table building and dependency resolution each parse their files independently; on subsequent runs the symbol index is loaded from the persisted cache and only the dependency pass re-parses. The per-file cost is the same; the peak WASM heap is now O(1) trees instead of O(files). Exit codes, relation-check semantics, and symbol-index caching are unchanged.

### Added

- **New deterministic aspect `wasm-tree-lifecycle` enforces correct use of `parseFile` from `ast/parser`.** Files that import `parseFile` directly are flagged unless they appear in a short allowlist of infrastructure modules that manage tree ownership explicitly (`ast/parser.ts`, `ast/runner.ts`, `relations/pass.ts`, `structure/ctx-parsers.ts`). All other code must use `withParsedFile`, which encodes the lifecycle guarantee in the API itself. The aspect is attached to the `cli` root node and cascades to all descendants (zero LLM cost — deterministic check only).

### Changed

- **Coverage scan and suppression scan now use disk-based file enumeration instead of `git ls-files`.** Both `yg check` (coverage/unmapped-file detection) and `yg suppressions` (suppress-marker scan) previously called `git ls-files` to enumerate files. That list includes files deleted from disk with `rm` but not `git rm` — such files appeared as unmapped even though they no longer existed. Both commands now use `walkRepoFiles`, which scans the filesystem directly with full `.gitignore` semantics (cascading nested `.gitignore` files, excludes `.git/` and `.yggdrasil/`). Files that do not exist on disk are invisible to both scans; gitignored files are excluded before they can reach coverage analysis, which also eliminates false `mapped-file-gitignored` detections for force-tracked gitignored files. The scans work correctly in repositories with no git history or in directories without a git repo at all.

## [5.2.5] - 2026-06-24

### Fixed

- **`yg check --approve --only-deterministic` no longer crashes with `Aborted()` on large repos.** web-tree-sitter `Parser` and `Tree` objects are backed by the WASM heap and require explicit `.delete()` to release memory — JavaScript GC does not free them. On repos with many files and aspects, hundreds of parsers and trees accumulated across a single `--approve` run and exhausted the WASM heap, triggering an internal `abort()` that surfaced as an unclassified `Aborted()` error after all pairs were verified. Two fixes: (1) `Parser` objects are now cached one per language rather than created fresh on every `parseFile()` call — Language objects were already cached, so the only allocation this eliminates is the per-call `new Parser()` that was immediately abandoned. (2) `Tree` objects are now explicitly freed when their owning scope exits — structure/runner and ast/runner destroy locally-created parse caches via `try/finally`, and the inline trees in the suppress-ranges, suppressions, and relations passes call `.delete()` after use.
- **A clean `yg check --approve` no longer hangs after the report — the success path now force-exits like the error and dry-run paths.** The command routed its exit through `exitAfterFlush` (which drains stdout and arms an unref'd 2-second force-exit backstop) only when there were errors; a clean run fell through to a bare `return` and relied on Node's event loop draining to exit. The fill stage opens LLM-provider handles — undici keep-alive sockets from the global `fetch`, per-request `AbortSignal.timeout` timers — that can outlive the work, so after a large successful fill (e.g. 120 reviewer calls all approved) the report printed but the process never exited. Both the `--approve` and the plain-check exit now go through `exitAfterFlush(hasErrors ? 1 : 0)` unconditionally, so the backstop guarantees the process exits within the grace window regardless of any lingering handle. Exit codes and output are unchanged (0 clean / 1 on errors); only the never-exits-on-success case is fixed. Plain `yg check` makes no reviewer calls so it never leaked a handle, but it is routed through the same single exit path so the guarantee can't regress in one branch while holding in the other.
- **`yg check` usage/option-validation errors now print to stderr, not stdout.** The four guarded-flag errors — `--top` with `--summary`, `--top`/`--summary` with `--approve`, an invalid `--top` value, and `--dry-run` without `--approve` — were written to stdout; they now go to stderr as the standard red what/why/next block, matching every other command and the CLI error-output contract. The exit code (1) and the message text are unchanged; only the stream differs. Adopters that captured these specific messages from stdout should read stderr instead.

## [5.2.3] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed

- **The "empty section ⇒ no file" rule now covers all three lock files, not just the logs file.** 5.2.2 stopped writing an empty `yg-lock.logs.json`; the two verdict files still wrote an empty `{ "version": 1, "verdicts": {}, "nodes": {} }` husk when they had nothing to record. Now every split file follows the same rule: `yg-lock.nondeterministic.json` is absent when there are no LLM verdicts, `.yg-lock.deterministic.json` (the gitignored cache) is absent when there are no deterministic verdicts, and `yg-lock.logs.json` is absent when there is no `log_required` source fingerprint and no `log.md` — in each case an existing empty husk is removed. `readLock` already treats an absent file as empty state, so this is transparent to every reader and changes no verdict, hash, or exit code; a repo simply carries only the lock files it actually needs. (Tests that assumed a verdict file is always present were updated to read whichever files exist.)

## [5.2.2] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed

- **The mandatory-log requirement is now enforced on plain `yg check`, not only at `--approve` — closing a hole where a `log_required` node with no pairs could change source silently.** Since the log gate moved to the fill stage, it was *pair-scoped*: `yg check --approve` built its gated node set from the unverified pairs to fill, so a `log_required` node that produced no pairs (every effective aspect `draft`, no effective aspects at all, or a source change touching only non-subject files) was never gated. Its source could change with no log entry and both plain `yg check` and `--approve` stayed green — the requirement had no teeth for that class of node, and the closure's refusal to advance the fingerprint was silent (no error surfaced). The requirement is now a property of the node TYPE plus a source change, fully decoupled from aspects and pairs, and enforced READ-ONLY: `yg check` computes each `log_required` node's source fingerprint live (like the relation-conformance check, at zero LLM cost) and emits a blocking `log-entry-missing` error when the mapped source drifted from the lock baseline (or is a first verification) with no fresh entry. So a CI run on plain `yg check` (or `--only-deterministic`) catches an unlogged source change even on a node with no pairs; `--approve` only writes the closure baseline once the entry exists, and its final re-check surfaces the same error. The new error outranks `unverified` in the `suggestedNext` cascade (adding the entry is the gating first step) and renders in the log-error group. Reuses the single source-of-truth gate predicate shared with the fill stage and positive closure (relocated into the shared read-only `core/log` module so the read path consults it without depending on the fill stage), so the three paths can never diverge. Unchanged: a node with no `mapping:` (no source) is never gated; `log_required: false` is a no-op; a cascade-only re-verification (aspect edited, source untouched) needs no new entry. The agent rules and the `log-management` / `working-with-architecture` / `cli-reference` knowledge topics were updated to describe read-only enforcement and the pair-independence of the requirement.
- **Positive closure no longer records a source fingerprint for non-`log_required` nodes — the committed `yg-lock.logs.json` stops accumulating dead per-node entries.** The `nodes.<path>.source` fingerprint is the log gate's drift basis, and the gate runs only for `log_required` nodes — yet closure recorded it for EVERY node that reached closure, so a repo could carry a source fingerprint (and churn it on every source change) for nodes the gate never consults. Closure now records `source` only for `log_required` nodes. A non-`log_required` node gets a `nodes` entry only when it owns a `log.md` — and then only the append-only `log` baseline (history integrity is independent of `log_required`), never a `source`; a stale fingerprint left by an earlier CLI is stripped, and an existing log baseline is preserved even if the `log.md` vanished so a deleted log is still caught as an integrity violation.
- **An empty `yg-lock.logs.json` is no longer written — when there is nothing to record, the file is absent.** When no node is `log_required` and none owns a `log.md`, the logs lock's `nodes` section is empty; rather than committing an empty `{ "version": 1, "verdicts": {}, "nodes": {} }` husk, `yg check --approve` now writes no file (and removes an existing empty one). `readLock` already treats an absent file as empty state, so this is transparent to every reader; the two verdict files (committed LLM verdicts, gitignored deterministic cache) are unaffected and still write their canonical cold-start shape.

## [5.2.1] - 2026-06-22

### Fixed

- **An unparseable reviewer reply is now dumped in full to `.debug.log` (under `debug: true`), so the failure can actually be diagnosed.** When a reviewer's response could not be parsed into a verdict, the failure was effectively invisible: the provider-error `reason` carried only the first 160 characters and the complete raw reply was never written anywhere, so an intermittent local-model failure — an empty `content` from a thinking model that spent its whole budget reasoning, a leaked reasoning wrapper, or a reply missing the `satisfied` field — left nothing to inspect. `parseAspectResponse` (the single choke point every provider funnels through, so the fix covers ollama and the CLI-subprocess providers at once) now writes the complete raw reply to the debug log when — and only when — parsing fails, and notes an empty reply distinctly. The logging stays gated on `debug: true` (these are private, opt-in local logs) and never fires on success (a parsed verdict logs nothing). No redaction primitive was introduced and the enforced `provider-redaction` rule is unaffected: the dump references the already-in-hand parse input (`output`), not the redaction-gated provider identifiers (`prompt`/`response`/`content`/`body`). This closes a diagnosis gap surfaced while investigating intermittent ollama parse failures; it does not change any verdict, hash, or exit code.

## [5.2.0] - 2026-06-21

### Added

- **`yg impact --node` / `--file` now reports the reviewer-call cost of an edit.** Where the node/file form previously printed a vague sentence with no number, it now shows a precise cost line — `Editing this {node|file} re-verifies: N LLM pair(s) = M reviewer call(s) (consensus included); D deterministic = free; G currently-green verdict(s) re-rolled.` — sourced from the owner node's expected pairs and the committed lock. `--file` scopes the estimate to the pairs whose subject set includes that file; `--node` covers the whole owner node. The reviewer-call count folds each LLM pair's resolved tier consensus, so an aspect on a consensus-3 tier shows three calls per unit. The cost machinery (previously wired only to `--aspect`) now answers the more common question — "what will editing this file cost?" — before you change anything.
- **`yg check --approve --dry-run` — a free cost preview of a repo-wide fill.** A new flag on `yg check --approve` that runs the same structural gate, pair classification, and reviewer-call budget computation a real fill would, prints the pre-dispatch header plus a per-node / per-aspect breakdown (each deterministic pair labelled `free`, each LLM pair labelled with its consensus call count), then exits 0 WITHOUT calling the reviewer, running any `check.mjs`, or writing a single byte to any lock file — the no-write guarantee is structural (the early-return precedes the serialized writer's construction). The reviewer-call total is framed as an UPPER BOUND: a node with an enforced deterministic refusal has its LLM fills skipped, and a fresh refusal or an infrastructure disposition can leave a pair unfilled, so the real `--approve` bills at most that many calls. The preview always exits 0, even when enforced pairs are unverified — it never blocks the build; only a broken configuration (the structural gate) aborts the preview, surfacing the same blocker a real `--approve` would hit. The preview intentionally bypasses the per-node log gate (a cost estimate must not demand a fresh log entry). `--dry-run` requires `--approve`; used on its own it is a guided usage error steering to `yg check --approve --dry-run` (plain `yg check` is already a free, no-write read).
- **`yg check --top [N]` and `yg check --summary` — read-only triage views for a large check wall.** Two new flags on the plain read narrow the output without losing fidelity. `--top N` prints only the N highest-priority issue blocks (in the same priority order the `Next:` line draws from); a bare `--top` (no value) prints zero blocks and keeps only the single suggested-next line. `--summary` prints one line per node — `K unverified (J deterministic-free, L LLM), M refused` — plus an `other` bucket for non-pair errors (coverage, log, relation, structural) so the per-node totals reconcile with the header. Guardrail: every view always prints the TRUE aggregate `Errors (N)` / `Warnings (N)` header and preserves the real exit code, so a narrowed view can never read as a clean build. An invalid `--top` value (negative, fractional, non-numeric, or an explicit `0`) is a guided error rather than a silent full dump; `--top` and `--summary` are mutually exclusive, and neither combines with `--approve` (which has its own `--dry-run` cost preview) — both rejected with guided what/why/next errors. Plain `yg check` (no flag) and the `--approve` report are unchanged (always full). These replace the lossy `| grep` / `| head` / `| tail` habit the rules warn against.
- **Meta-modeling — model and verify the graph's own rule files.** A new `yg knowledge read meta-modeling` topic (and a matching docs-site page) documents mapping files under `.yggdrasil/` into the graph so the rules can review the rules: the four ways a rule file reaches a reviewer (mapping → subject, static `references:`, per-unit `companion.mjs`, deterministic `ctx.fs`), the consequences (mapped checks are parsed by the built-in relation check and reviewed by every effective aspect; never map `.yggdrasil/**` wholesale — it would sweep in the committed lock), and how to keep the blast radius small. This is documentation over behaviour that already worked — no engine change — backed by end-to-end tests proving the mapping / reference / companion / `ctx.fs` channels reach a `.yggdrasil/` check and that removing the relation fails closed ("no relation → error").
- **Aspect-authoring guidance: directory organization + positive vs negative rules.** `yg knowledge read aspects-overview` (and the Aspects doc) now explain that aspect ids may be nested in directories (a directory with no rule file is a pure organizational grouper; nesting is naming, not cascade), and that an aspect may be positive (something must be present) or negative (something must not be) — including the broad-negative-with-carve-out pattern: attach a prohibition at a parent and exclude the one node type allowed to do the forbidden thing via `when: { not: { node: { type: ... } } }`.

### Fixed

- **A reviewer tier that OMITS `max_prompt_chars` now caps the assembled prompt at 50000 instead of being unlimited.** The prompt-size gate was skipped entirely when a tier left `max_prompt_chars` out, so a hand-authored tier without the key had no ceiling and could send arbitrarily large prompts to the reviewer — risking context-window truncation and a false verdict. An omitted key now defaults to 50000 (the same value `yg init` already writes), applied at both the read-only verify gate and the companion first-fill gate; the parser still records `undefined` when the key is absent (the default is gate-applied only, so no verdict re-roll). The gate is `chars > limit`, so a prompt of exactly 50000 passes and 50001 trips. **Adopter consequence:** a tier that previously relied on the absent key for unlimited prompts will now report `prompt-too-large` for any pair whose assembled prompt exceeds 50000 — set `max_prompt_chars` explicitly to a higher value (or move the aspect to a higher-capability tier) if that ceiling is too low.
- **`prompt-too-large` over-counted companion-bearing LLM aspects on plain `yg check`.** The read-only size gate reconstructed a companion aspect's prompt from the stored `touched` read-keys, which conflate the files a `companion.mjs` hook merely READ to decide with the files it actually INJECTED — so a hook that read broadly (e.g. via `ctx.graph`) was measured at the size of its whole reachable set and falsely flagged `prompt-too-large`, even though `--dry-run` and the fill stage (which assemble the real companions) measured it correctly. `yg check` now resolves the companion LIVE — the same resolver the fill and `--dry-run` use — and measures only the injected files. A companion that fails to resolve during the gate surfaces a clear diagnostic naming the aspect, the unit, and the hook's error (instead of a generic unverified). Plain `yg check` may now run `companion.mjs` (the dependency resolver, never a judge) when a tier sets a size limit; it still never runs `check.mjs` and never calls a reviewer. An over-limit companion prompt is now caught at the check/verify stage even on a first fill (reviewer never billed, nothing written), rather than only at fill time.
- **Log-recovery guidance now names the committed log-baseline file, not the retired single-file lock.** When `yg check` flags a broken append-only log (`log-integrity`), the restore-from-git instruction — both the per-issue `next` and the trailing `suggestedNext` — pointed at `.yggdrasil/yg-lock.json`, a file that no longer exists since the lock was split into a committed/gitignored triad. Following that guidance silently `git checkout`s a path that resolves to nothing, leaving the actual baseline untouched. The guidance now names `.yggdrasil/yg-lock.logs.json` (where the per-node log/closure baseline lives), and the two knowledge topics that referenced the zombie name were corrected to the right triad member (`yg-lock.logs.json` for the log baseline, `yg-lock.nondeterministic.json` for the LLM verdict store). A regression test now asserts every lock path emitted in a recovery string resolves to a git-tracked file, so a reintroduced bare `yg-lock.json` fails the suite. Docs-and-strings only; no check logic changed.
- **A `log.md` left with git conflict markers now routes to `yg log merge-resolve` instead of a misleading hand-edit instruction.** When a merge left unresolved conflict markers in a node's `log.md`, `yg check` mis-reported it as a generic `log-format` (or `log-integrity`) error whose `next` told the agent to fix the file by hand — but hand-stitching the two sides breaks the append-only integrity hashes and leaves `yg check` red. `yg check` now detects the conflict FIRST and emits a dedicated `log-conflict` error whose `next` is `yg log merge-resolve --node <path>` (the only safe reconciliation), and `suggestedNext` routes there too (it outranks both log-integrity and log-format in the priority cascade). Detection keys off the unambiguous open/close markers only (a line starting with seven `<` or seven `>`); a bare `=======` line is deliberately NOT a trigger, since in a markdown log it is a legitimate setext H1 underline / horizontal rule and would false-positive — a documented deviation from the JSON-lock conflict check, which has no such ambiguity.
- **`yg-suppress` now scopes a marker identically for LLM and deterministic aspects — the reviewer prompt no longer re-interprets marker scope.** The deterministic matcher has always resolved `yg-suppress` markers into exact line ranges (single-line = the one line below the marker; `disable`/`enable` = the bracketed span; a bare `disable` = through end of file), but the LLM reviewer prompt told the model to self-interpret scope ("applies contextually to the surrounding code (function, class, or block)… at file level, the entire file"). So the same marker could waive one line for a deterministic check and a whole function — or whole file — for an LLM aspect on the same code. The prompt now INJECTS the pre-resolved spans into a deterministic `<suppressed-ranges>` block and instructs the reviewer to honor exactly those lines without re-deriving the marker's scope, so both reviewer kinds waive the identical lines. A single-line marker no longer reads as whole-file for an LLM aspect — use the bracket form to waive a range. The prompt is byte-identical when a unit has no applicable markers (no verdict re-roll: the resolved ranges are not a verdict-hash input), and a reasonless marker fails closed (no reviewer call, nothing written) just as it does on the deterministic path. The `yg knowledge read suppress-syntax` topic and the agent rules were updated to describe the unified behavior.
- **The configuration docs no longer promise an unknown-key error for retired `quality.*` / dead `config.*` fields.** The "Upgrading" guidance (both `yg knowledge read configuration` and the docs-site page) claimed a leftover `quality.max_node_chars` or a per-tier `references:`/`config.context_length_field` cap would fail `yg check` with a clear unknown-key error — but the parser reads only the keys it recognizes and SILENTLY IGNORES the rest (no error, no warning), so that promise was false and an adopter waiting for the error to flag the dead lines would never see it. The docs now state the truth: retired `quality.*` fields and unknown `config.*` keys under a tier are silently dropped — review the diff after `yg init --upgrade` and delete them by hand. The parser's genuine unknown-KEY guard is unchanged and clarified as distinct: a typo'd top-level key under `reviewer:` or a tier still fails with `config-reviewer-unknown-key` / `config-tier-unknown-key`. The dead `config.context_length_field` row was also removed from the per-tier field table (the parser never reads it). Docs-and-tests only; no parser change.

## [5.1.1] - 2026-06-20

### Fixed

- **`yg init` wrote the reviewer API key to a location that broke the next `yg check` — and the key was never actually used.** For every provider that requires an API key (`openai`, `anthropic`, `google`, `openai-compatible`), `yg init` (and the "Change reviewer" reconfigure flow) wrote the key into `yg-secrets.yaml` at a provider-level bucket — `reviewer.<provider>.api_key`. But `yg-secrets.yaml` is a 1:1 deep-merge overlay over `yg-config.yaml`, where the `reviewer:` section accepts only `default` and `tiers`; after the overlay merged, the very next `yg check` failed to parse the config with `config-reviewer-unknown-key` ("unknown key '<provider>' under reviewer:"). The key now lands where the schema, the docs, and the overlay contract already say it belongs: inside the bootstrapped tier's `config:` block — `reviewer.tiers.standard.config.api_key`. Separately, the config parser never copied `config.api_key` out of a tier into the resolved reviewer configuration, so even a hand-placed, correctly-shaped key was silently dropped and only the `*_API_KEY` environment-variable fallback ever worked; the parser now plumbs the tier's `api_key` through. The key stays excluded from the verdict hash (only the tier name is an input), so configuring or rotating a key invalidates no recorded verdicts.

## [5.1.0] - 2026-06-20

### Added

- **`yg schemas` — a built-in browser for the graph-element schema references.** `yg schemas list` lists the five schemas (`node`, `aspect`, `architecture`, `config`, `flow`); `yg schemas read <name>` prints one schema's field reference. The content is embedded in the CLI and resolves without a project graph, exactly like `yg knowledge` — so the reference for any graph element is one command away, in any directory, and always matches the installed CLI.

- **Per-unit companion files for LLM aspects (`companion.mjs`).** An LLM aspect may now ship an optional `companion.mjs` alongside `content.md`. For each verification unit the runner executes `export async function companion(ctx)`, which resolves 0–N read-only companion files from relation-reachable nodes, and injects those files into that unit's reviewer prompt only — in a distinct `<companions>` block, separate from static `references:`. The hook returns `Array<{ path, label? }>` (paths only, never file content); the runner reads and injects each file. Use this to feed the reviewer exactly one paired counterpart per unit (a scenario document with its test spec, a migration with its schema) without embedding an entire related node in every prompt. `companion.mjs` is an add-on to LLM aspects only — it does not change reviewer-kind inference; `companion.mjs` without `content.md` (`aspect-companion-without-content`) and `companion.mjs` alongside `check.mjs` (`aspect-companion-with-check`) are blocking validator errors. Any failure to assemble a companion (hook throws, bad return shape, missing path, path outside allowed reads, import error) is an infra-fail: nothing is written, the pair stays unverified, and `yg check` stays red — consistent with how a failing `check.mjs` behaves. `yg-suppress` markers in companion files are ignored; suppression is scoped to the subject source files only. The verdict hash gains two optional ingredients folded only when present — `companionHash` (SHA-256 of `companion.mjs`, present whenever the aspect ships it) and `touched` (the hook's observations, folded only when non-empty) — so a plain LLM aspect's hash is byte-identical to before: no lock-format change, no schema-version bump, no migration. Editing `companion.mjs` re-verifies every pair of the aspect (billed); editing a resolved companion file re-verifies only the pairs that read it (also billed, reflected by `yg impact --file`). `yg aspect-test --dry-run` runs the hook live and shows resolved companions without making any reviewer call or touching the lock.

- **`yg check --approve --only-deterministic` — the cheap, keyless CI / pre-commit gate.** A new flag on `yg check --approve` that fills ONLY the deterministic (`check.mjs`) pairs — no reviewer, no API key, no cost — and writes ONLY the gitignored deterministic cache, never the committed lock files (positive closure is skipped and garbage-collection is scoped to the cache, so a CI run produces zero committed-lock churn). It still re-hashes the committed LLM verdicts in the trailing report, so it catches both a deterministic regression and a stale committed LLM verdict in one pass. Use it in CI and the pre-commit hook in place of plain `yg check`: a fresh checkout has no deterministic cache (it is gitignored), so those pairs read as unverified until this rematerializes them (free).

### Changed

- **The verdict lock is split into a committed/gitignored triad — deterministic verdicts are no longer committed.** The single `.yggdrasil/yg-lock.json` becomes three files: `yg-lock.nondeterministic.json` (committed — LLM verdicts) and `yg-lock.logs.json` (committed — the per-node log/closure baseline), plus `.yg-lock.deterministic.json` (gitignored — deterministic-check verdicts). Deterministic verdicts are a pure local performance cache: committing them added roughly half the lock's bytes and merge noise while contributing nothing to a clone that can recompute them for free. The in-memory lock is unchanged (`{ version, verdicts, nodes }`); the split is only at the I/O boundary, partitioned by aspect KIND (not by the entry's `touched` field — a companion-bearing LLM entry also carries `touched`, so kind is the only safe key). The committed files keep the same take-a-side merge story; the gitignored cache never conflicts. CI and pre-commit move to `yg check --approve --only-deterministic` (keyless, free) — plain `yg check` on a fresh checkout reports deterministic pairs as unverified until the cache is rematerialized. **Migration:** `yg init --upgrade` advances the graph to 5.1.0 and splits an existing `yg-lock.json` in place, relocating every verdict verbatim (content-addressed — zero re-verification, no LLM cost) and adding the cache to `.yggdrasil/.gitignore`.

- **Schema references are no longer per-project files.** The `.yggdrasil/schemas/` directory is gone — schema field references now live in the CLI and are read via `yg schemas read <name>` instead of opening `schemas/<file>.yaml`. A fresh `yg init` no longer creates the directory, and `yg init --upgrade` removes an existing one. Every place that pointed at a schema file — the agent rules, the knowledge topics, the docs, and the CLI's own diagnostics — now points at the command. **Migration:** run `yg init --upgrade` once; it advances the graph schema version to 5.1.0 and deletes the obsolete `schemas/` directory. Existing projects keep working until then — the directory is inert.

### Removed

- **The schema-presence check (`schema-missing`) is gone.** Schemas were only ever validated by filename presence, never by content; with the files removed from the project graph there is nothing to require, so `yg check` no longer reports or needs a `schemas/` directory.

## [5.0.4] - 2026-06-18

### Fixed

- **`yg` no longer aborts at shutdown on Windows with a libuv assertion (`UV_HANDLE_CLOSING`, exit 127).** Commands that run a deterministic check — `yg aspect-test`, and `yg check --approve` while filling deterministic pairs — import each `check.mjs` through a `node:module` customization hook, which Node runs on a worker thread connected by a MessagePort (a libuv async handle). The shared command-exit helper then called `process.exit()` the instant the command finished, tearing the event loop down while that handle was still live; on a Windows debug-assert libuv build (observed on Node 24) that tripped `assert(!(handle->flags & UV_HANDLE_CLOSING))` in `src\win\async.c` and aborted the process with exit 127 — intermittently leaving a `--approve` fill incomplete so it had to be re-run. The helper now sets `process.exitCode` and returns to the event loop, letting Node shut the hook worker down cleanly before the process exits, with an unref'd fallback that still force-exits if some other handle ever keeps the loop alive past a grace window. Output is still drained before exit, so large reports piped to another command are never truncated. No behavior change on macOS/Linux, where the same teardown was already silent (libuv assertions compiled out).

## [5.0.3] - 2026-06-18

### Fixed

- **CLI reviewers (`claude-code`, `codex`, `gemini-cli`) are no longer reported as missing on Windows.** Detection of whether a provider's command is installed shelled out to `which <binary>` — but `which` is a Unix-only command. On Windows there is no `which` on PATH (the equivalent is `where`), so the probe failed to spawn `which` itself and concluded the binary was absent on every Windows machine, even when the command was installed and runnable. As a result `yg init` always warned "`claude` not found on PATH" and the reviewer's runtime availability gate (`isAvailable()`) always returned false, so a correctly-installed CLI reviewer could not be used at all. Detection now runs the binary directly with `--version` and treats a clean exit as "available" — no platform-specific lookup tool, so it works identically on Windows, macOS, and Linux. On Windows the probe runs through the shell so PATHEXT shims (e.g. a `claude.cmd` installed by npm) resolve; the binary name is always a fixed internal constant, never user input. Running the binary also confirms it actually executes, which a bare path lookup never verified.

## [5.0.2] - 2026-06-17

### Fixed

- **Line-ending style no longer affects verdicts.** Content is now hashed with line endings normalized (CRLF and lone CR collapse to LF) before the SHA-256 fold. The same file checked out with CRLF on Windows and LF on Linux produces an identical verdict hash and source fingerprint, so a mixed-platform team no longer sees pairs flip to unverified — or the mandatory-log gate re-trigger — purely because of a line-ending difference. Only the line-break style is normalized; any other byte change (including trailing whitespace) still invalidates as before.

## [5.0.1] - 2026-06-16

### Changed

- **The mandatory-log gate now stops `yg check --approve` entirely instead of partially filling.** When any node whose source changed lacks a fresh log entry, `--approve` emits the per-node "add a log entry" messages and stops — approving nothing and not printing the check report. So a run is now either a clean approval followed by the check result, or a clear "add these entries first" halt. Previously it skipped the gated nodes, filled the rest, and printed a report mixing both, which obscured what was actually needed.
- **A verdict's reviewer identity is the tier NAME alone.** The LLM verdict hash folds only the resolved tier's name — not its config (provider, model, endpoint, temperature, consensus). Re-pointing a named tier at a different reviewer, or overriding it locally, does not invalidate recorded baselines. Only `api_key` and `timeout` were excluded before; now the entire tier config is.
- **`yg-secrets.yaml` is a deep-merge overlay over `yg-config.yaml`.** It mirrors the full config shape and overrides any field locally — most often a tier's `api_key`, provider, model, or endpoint — not just `api_key`. The api_key-only restriction and the `secrets-non-credential-field` validator are removed. **Migration:** move local secrets from the old `reviewer.<provider>.api_key` shape to the config-mirroring `reviewer.tiers.<name>.config.api_key`.

### Fixed

- **Reviewer replies that are real verdicts but malformed JSON are now salvaged instead of failing closed.** The shared `parseAspectResponse` (used by every provider) now recovers a verdict from a reply carrying a `"satisfied": true|false` field even when the JSON is invalid — an unescaped `"` inside the `reason`, a truncated/unclosed `reason`, chain-of-thought leaked into the `reason`, or a quoted `"true"`/`"false"` value. The verdict is taken from the field (never from a bare word in prose, so a garbled reply is still classified as an infra error — no false PASS). A reference catalogue of well-formed and malformed replies under `reference/llm-aspect-verdict-responses/` (one `.md` per case, like the relations name-resolution catalogue — governed by `_reference-schema.yaml` and the generic `reference/doc-shape` + `reference/layout` aspects) drives the tests; add a `.md` to add a case. This mainly unblocks local/quantized models (e.g. Ollama) that don't reliably honor `format: json`.
- **Interrupting `yg check --approve` now preserves every finished LLM verdict.** Each reviewer verdict is written to the lock the moment its pair completes — the same way deterministic checks already behaved — so Ctrl+C keeps all completed work and the next run resumes only the remainder. Previously LLM verdicts were buffered in memory and flushed only after the whole tier's batch finished, so an interrupt discarded every completed reviewer call (the most expensive work to lose).
- **`yg init` no longer loops on "upgrade required" after a version-only release.** The graph version is the schema version — it advances only when the graph format changes — so `yg init` compares it against the schema version the CLI supports, not the package version. A patch release that leaves the graph format unchanged needs no upgrade.
- **The Ollama reviewer runs with the model's native thinking enabled.** A reasoning model previously had thinking turned off, so it had to emit the `satisfied` verdict as the opening token of the response JSON — before working through the rules — and on harder files that snap judgment could contradict the model's own reasoning (a refusal whose explanation concluded the code was fine). Reasoning now runs in the model's separate thinking channel and only the final verdict JSON is parsed, so the verdict follows the reasoning and chain-of-thought never leaks into the parsed reason. Because a thinking pass can run for minutes, the Ollama request timeout is raised from the shared 60s default to a generous ceiling (5 min), overridable per tier via `timeout`.
- **The `yg init` Ollama connection probe no longer reports a false failure on a working large local model.** It now disables the model's reasoning trace — the probe only confirms the model answers, so it skips the reasoning pass — and allows 60s for a cold model load, instead of a 15s cap that a large model's first load from disk could exceed.

## [5.0.0] - 2026-06-15

Stable release — the 5.0.0 line is closed. The changes that make up 5.0.0 (the
content-addressed verdict lock and `yg check --approve`, deterministic and LLM
reviewers, aspect `scope` and `status`, live relation-conformance, glob mapping,
and scoped coverage) are documented across the `5.0.0-alpha.*` pre-releases below;
this section records what changed since the final alpha.

### Changed

- **User docs restructured around the "rails" model.** The documentation site is now a guided funnel: a rebuilt home page and a new "How it works" page lead with the mental model (you lay the rails, the agent drives, the reviewer keeps it on track); the monolithic "Core Concepts" page is split into focused **Aspects**, **Nodes**, and **Relations, flows & ports** pages; and a new **The lock** page collects the verdict-lock mechanics out of the concept pages. Navigation is regrouped (Start here / Build the rails / Author & operate / Reference & deep dives) and the dogfood showcase is marked advanced.
- **README, npm package description, and package README rebalanced toward the value adopters report.** They now lead with the two things that consistently deliver — scoped rules delivered to the agent *before* it writes (prevention), and the deterministic checks that run on every change and can't be quietly optimized away — and frame the LLM reviewer as the higher-variance judgment layer to keep small and stage through `advisory`. Two honesty notes added: rules enforce structure, not runtime behavior, and a green check is only as good as the rule behind it. The hero demo GIF (`tools/render-demo-gif.js`) was re-cut to match (prevention + the un-ignorable deterministic/relation gate as the spine; the LLM refusal as one beat).

### Fixed

- **Reviewer verdicts are no longer truncated by an output-token cap.** The Anthropic (`max_tokens: 500`) and Ollama (`num_predict: 500`) reviewers capped generation low enough that a long verdict — the refusal reason, or the verdict JSON itself — could be clipped mid-response, arriving truncated and, at worst, unparseable, costing a wasted re-verification. After a manual audit of every provider: Anthropic's ceiling is raised far above any verdict (the API requires one), Ollama now generates unbounded (`num_predict: -1`), and the OpenAI / OpenAI-compatible, Google, and the three CLI providers (`claude-code`, `codex`, `gemini-cli`) were confirmed to impose no output cap at all. A reply that still arrives truncated continues to fail closed in parsing rather than be trusted as a verdict. (The init-time connectivity probe keeps its tiny `max_tokens: 10` — it only pings the provider, it does not verify code.)

## [5.0.0-alpha.6] - 2026-06-15

### Added

- **TypeScript relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a TypeScript / TSX / JavaScript file declares (static `import`, re-export with a source, `import x = require(...)`, `require(...)`, and string-literal dynamic `import(...)`), resolves each relative specifier to its owning node, and refuses a node that depends on another node without a declared relation — reported as `relation-undeclared-dependency` naming the importing file and the target node. Whole-statement `import type`, dynamic imports of a non-literal specifier, bare/external packages, intra-node imports, dependencies onto an ancestor node, and imports that resolve to an unmapped file are all silent by design (no waiver needed).
- **Python relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a Python file declares through an import statement — plain `import a.b.c` (alias-stripped: `import x as y` records the real module `x`), and `from M import …` in both absolute (`from a.b import c`) and relative (`from . import sib`, `from ..pkg.mod import y`) forms, including `from pkg import *` (one edge for `pkg`). Each module path is resolved to its owning node by mirroring CPython's package layout — probing the importing file's directory and every ancestor source root for `<module>.py` / `<module>/__init__.py`, with a longest-match fallback so `from pkg import sub` resolves to `pkg/sub.py` or to `pkg`'s `__init__.py` — then refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers the target. Class bases, decorators, and call sites are usage-site relation-type refinement and are deferred (the import is the dependency edge). `from __future__ import …`, dynamic `importlib.import_module(...)`, stdlib/third-party imports, intra-node imports, dependencies onto an ancestor node, and imports that resolve to an unmapped file are all silent by design (no waiver needed); any module that does not resolve to exactly one mapped file is a non-event.
- **Go relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a Go file declares through an import — single (`import "path"`) and grouped (`import ( … )`) forms alike, with the import PATH as the edge (the local binding is irrelevant, so aliased `pay "…"`, blank `_ "…"`, dot `. "…"`, and raw-string backtick paths all record the same package path). Because a Go import names a package DIRECTORY, each path is resolved by reading the module path from the nearest `go.mod`, stripping that prefix to get the repo-relative package directory, and picking a representative `.go` file in it (production files preferred over `*_test.go`) — that file's owning node is the dependency target, refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers it. Selector calls (`pkg.Func`), package-qualified types, and struct/interface embedding are usage-site relation-type refinement and are deferred (the import is the dependency edge). Stdlib and external-module imports (anything not under the module path), imports to an unmapped in-module package, intra-node imports, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); a missing or unreadable `go.mod` degrades to silence rather than guessing.
- **Java relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a Java file declares through an `import` — single-type (`import com.acme.PaymentService;`), static (`import static com.acme.Helpers.format;`, off-by-one stripped so the dependency is the type `com.acme.Helpers`), and wildcard (`import com.acme.audit.*;`, recorded as the PACKAGE edge) forms, with the import as the dependency edge. Each fully-qualified name is resolved by Java's package = directory convention — probing the importing file's directory and every ancestor source root for `com/acme/Type.java` (with a nested-type longest-match fallback so `com.acme.Outer.Inner` resolves to `Outer.java`), and a wildcard package to any `.java` in its directory — then refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers the target node. A same-package (bare-name) reference and an expression-position dotted access (a fully-qualified static call `com.acme.Helpers.format()`) refine the relation type or do not name a type, so they add no separate edge; a fully-qualified inline TYPE reference (`extends`/`implements` super-types, field/parameter/return/local types, generic arguments, `new`, `catch`) is itself detected (see the inline-reference detection under Changed). JDK (`java.*` / `javax.*` / `jakarta.*`) and external-library imports, reflective `Class.forName(...)` string references, imports to an unmapped `.java` file, intra-node imports, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); any FQN that does not resolve to a mapped `.java` is a non-event.
- **PHP relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a PHP file declares through a top-level `use` import — plain (`use App\Payment\Gateway;`), aliased (`use App\Payment\Gateway as G;`, the real fully-qualified name recorded, never the alias), grouped (`use App\Payment\{Charge, Refund as R};`, expanded into one edge per imported class), and leading-backslash (`use \App\Payment\Gateway;`, the backslash stripped) forms, with the import as the dependency edge. Each fully-qualified name is resolved through composer's PSR-4 autoloading — the nearest ancestor `composer.json`'s `autoload.psr-4` / `autoload-dev.psr-4` maps a namespace prefix to a base directory, and the longest matching prefix turns the remaining namespace segments into a `.php` file path — then refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers the target node. When the target is reached through a `use` import these usage-site constructs (`extends`/`implements`, in-body trait `use`, `new`, static calls, type hints, `instanceof`, attributes) only refine the relation type (not enforced in v1) and add no separate edge; a LEADING-BACKSLASH (absolute) inline reference in those positions is itself detected as an edge (see the inline-reference detection under Changed), while a namespace-relative inline reference stays silent. `use function` / `use const` imports, vendor / third-party classes (a namespace outside the project's PSR-4 map), projects autoloaded by classmap / files instead of PSR-4, a missing or unreadable `composer.json`, dynamic instantiation (`new $class()`), imports to an unmapped `.php` file, intra-node imports, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); any FQN that does not resolve to a mapped `.php` is a non-event.
- **Kotlin relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a Kotlin file declares through an `import` — plain (`import com.acme.PaymentService`), aliased (`import com.acme.Helpers as H`, the real fully-qualified name recorded, never the alias), and wildcard (`import com.acme.audit.*`, recorded as the PACKAGE edge) forms, with the import as the dependency edge. Kotlin is the FIRST language resolved through a SYMBOL TABLE rather than a path mapping: because a Kotlin file's `package` is decoupled from its directory (and `.kts` scripts may have no package at all), each file's fully-qualified names are read from its parsed `package_header` plus each top-level/nested declaration's simple name (`class` / `interface` / `object` / `fun` / `val` / `typealias`), forming `<package>.<Name>` keys folded into one shared symbol table; an import's fully-qualified name then resolves to its uniquely-declaring file's owning node and is refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers it. `by` delegation, expression-position qualified calls, and `::` callable references add no separate edge (the import is the existence edge); a fully-qualified inline TYPE reference (a supertype, a parameter/return/property type, a generic argument, an `is`/`as` type, a type-alias RHS) is itself detected (see the inline-reference detection under Changed). Kotlin/Java/AndroidX/third-party imports (`kotlin.*`, `kotlinx.*`, `java.*`), wildcard star-imports (which resolve to no single declared symbol in v1), reflective string-class references, imports to an unmapped `.kt` file, intra-node imports, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); a fully-qualified name declared by two or more files is ambiguous and resolves to a non-event rather than a flag.
- **C and C++ relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a C (`.c`/`.h`) or C++ (`.cpp`/`.hpp`/`.cc`/`.cxx`/`.hh`/`.hxx`) file declares through a QUOTED preprocessor include (`#include "db/connection.h"`) — the one parse-time reference these languages expose that names a concrete in-repo file. Both languages share a single include resolver: a quoted include resolves first relative to the including file's directory (the canonical meaning, e.g. `#include "../inc/foo.h"`), then against a few common include roots discovered by walking up the includer's ancestor directories (each ancestor and its `include/` subdirectory), and the resolved HEADER file's owning node is the dependency target — refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers it (a header and its implementation should share a node). Function calls (which bind at LINK time, and are AST-indistinguishable from function-like macros), class inheritance, namespace-qualified references, and `using` declarations are usage-site / definition-index work and are deferred (the quoted include is the dependency edge). Angle-bracket system includes (`#include <stdio.h>`), macro includes (`#include HDR`, no literal path), includes that resolve to an unmapped header, intra-node includes (a `.c`/`.cpp` including its own node's `.h`/`.hpp`), and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); the `.h` extension binds to the C grammar (not cpp), so a C++ header named `Foo.h` parses under C and its `#include` lines are still detected, with header-declared inheritance/namespaces a documented coverage gap.
- **C# relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a C# (`.cs`) file declares through a SYMBOL USE — a fully-qualified construction (`new MyApp.Payments.Gateway()`), a qualified base type or field/return/parameter type (`: MyApp.Web.BaseController`, `MyApp.Payments.IGateway _gw;`), or a bare type used in a base list or `new` that an in-file `using Foo.Bar;` brings into scope — with the type dependency, not an import statement, as the edge. C# is the design's poster child for SYMBOL-TABLE resolution and the HARDEST language: a `using` names a NAMESPACE (which spans arbitrarily many files in arbitrarily many directories — namespace is decoupled from directory) and never a file, so there is no syntactic file-to-file edge. Each file's declared types are read from its parsed namespace (block `namespace Foo { }`, nested, or file-scoped `namespace Foo.Bar;`) plus each `class` / `interface` / `struct` / `record` / `enum` name, forming `<Namespace>.<Type>` keys folded into one shared symbol table; a use's candidate fully-qualified name then resolves to its uniquely-declaring file's owning node and is refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers it. A bare name is qualified against every in-file `using` prefix and any `using Alias = …;` alias, and resolves ONLY when exactly one candidate uniquely matches a declared type — so several candidates for one bare name is safe (the rest are non-events). Method-call relation-type classification (calls vs uses vs extends/implements) is deferred; v1 establishes only that a type dependency exists. Dependency-injection container registration, reflection (`Type.GetType` / `Activator.CreateInstance`), extension methods, source-generated and `partial` types, `using static` member imports, a `global using` declared in another file, external-assembly / BCL types (`System.*`, `Microsoft.*`), imports to an unmapped `.cs` file, intra-node references, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); a fully-qualified name declared by two or more files is ambiguous and resolves to a non-event rather than a flag.
- **Rust relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts every cross-node dependency a Rust file declares through a `use` import — single (`use crate::payments::charge;`), renamed (`use crate::db::Repository as Repo;`, the real path recorded, never the alias), grouped (`use crate::orders::{Order, sub::Deep};`, recorded as the common module prefix `crate::orders` since both items resolve to the same module file/node), glob (`use crate::events::*;`, the prefix module edge), and `pub use` re-export forms, with the `use` import as the dependency edge. Each `::`-path is resolved through the crate MODULE TREE: the crate root is the `src/` directory of the nearest ancestor `Cargo.toml`, a `crate::a::b`-rooted path maps to `<src>/a/b.rs` or `<src>/a/b/mod.rs` (with a longest-match item fallback so `crate::a::Type` resolves to `<src>/a.rs` when the last segment is an item, not a module), and `super::` / `self::` resolve relative to the importing file's own module — then refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers the target node. A bare-name (non-`crate`/`self`/`super`-rooted) trait impl, supertrait bound, call, type reference, or struct literal refines the relation type or names a `use`-bound alias the tool must not guess, so it adds no separate edge; a `crate`/`self`/`super`-rooted inline path (type or expression position) and a file-backed `mod foo;` are themselves detected (see the inline-reference detection under Changed). Paths rooted at an external crate (`std::*`, `serde::*`, any first segment that is neither `crate`/`super`/`self` nor the crate's own package name), macro-generated dependencies (a path inside a `macro_invocation` token tree is never a `use`), imports to an unmapped `.rs` file, intra-node imports, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed); any path that does not resolve to a mapped `.rs` is a non-event.
- **Ruby relation conformance is live.** The relation pass now extracts cross-node dependencies a Ruby (`.rb`) file declares, using BOTH a PATH link and a CONSTANT index. The path link is `require_relative '<literal>'` — Ruby's one file-precise static reference — resolved relative to the requiring file's directory (`.rb` appended) to the target file's owning node. The constant index covers a superclass (`class C < Base`), a mixin (`include` / `extend` / `prepend Mod`), a `Foo::Bar` scope resolution, a constant receiver of a qualified call (`Payments::Gateway.charge`), and a bare constant used as a value (`x = Helper`): each file's `class` / `module` definitions and top-level constant assignments build fully-qualified `App::Services::OrderService` keys (from lexical nesting) folded into one shared symbol table, and a constant reference resolves to its uniquely-declaring file's owning node — refused as `relation-undeclared-dependency` when no declared relation covers it. Relation-type classification is deferred (v1 establishes only that a dependency exists). HONEST RECALL: `require_relative` is precise, but constants are mostly SILENCED by Ruby's pervasive class/module REOPENING — every reopening adds another definition of the same name, so the constant becomes ambiguous in the symbol table and resolves to a non-event rather than a flag. Plain `require` of a gem (only `require_relative` is a path link), dynamic / interpolated `require_relative` arguments, Zeitwerk / Rails autoload (a constant used with no `require`), `const_get` / `send` metaprogramming (a dynamic string, never a constant node), monkey-patching, external gems and stdlib constants, references to an unmapped `.rb` file, intra-node references, and dependencies onto an ancestor node are all silent by design (no waiver needed) — zero false positives by design, trading recall for soundness.
- **`relation-undeclared-dependency` now renders its full violation detail.** The refusal block lists each `<file>:<line> → undeclared dependency on <node>` under the header instead of truncating to the summary line, so the importing file and target are visible in plain `yg check` (matching the aspect-violation refusal rendering).

### Changed

- **Relation conformance now detects INLINE fully-qualified references (the provably zero-false-positive subset) in PHP, Java, Kotlin, and Rust — not just imports.** A real cross-component dependency written without an import is now an edge when, and only when, the reference is unambiguous by construction.
